V_{p_{1}},V_{p_{2}},V_{p_{3}}
. The analysis leads to the shift relations (
199
).

For the second term (i.e. the sum over the three remaining points) it is obvious what the residues are. For the first term involving the degenerate field, we will have contributions up to, and including, order
(z-z_{0})^{0}
in the OPE, since this term is
L_{-2}V_{p_{\langle 2,1\rangle}}
and should be replaced by
-\frac{1}{b^{2}}L_{-1}^{2}V_{p_{\langle 2,1\rangle}}=-\frac{1}{b^{2}}\partial_{z_{0}}^{2}V_{p_{\langle 2,1\rangle}}(z_{0})
from (
182
). Doing all that, we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg\{\prod_{i=1}^{3}(z_{0}-z_{i})

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{1}{b^{2}}\partial_{z_{0}}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{\partial_{z_{0}}}{z_{0}-z_{i}}\right)+(3z_{0}-z_{1}-z_{2}-z_{3})h_{\langle 2,1\rangle}

\displaystyle+\frac{z_{12}z_{13}}{z_{1}-z_{0}}h_{1}+\frac{z_{21}z_{23}}{z_{2}-z_{0}}h_{2}+\frac{z_{31}z_{32}}{z_{3}-z_{0}}h_{3}\bigg\}\langle V_{p_{\langle 2,1\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle=0\ .

(186)

We next write the correlation function in terms of the conformally invariant cross-ratio
z\equiv\frac{z_{01}z_{23}}{z_{03}z_{21}}
(with
z_{ij}=z_{i}-z_{j}
)

\langle V_{p_{\langle 2,1\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle=\left[\prod_{i<j}(z_{i}-z_{j})^{\mu_{ij}}\right]\mathcal{F}(z)

(187)

with
\mu_{ij}\equiv\frac{1}{3}\sum_{k=0}^{3}h_{k}-h_{i}-h_{j}
.
Taking the limits
(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})\rightarrow(0,1,\infty)
and rescaling

\mathcal{F}(z)=z^{-\mu_{01}}(1-z)^{-\mu_{02}}\mathcal{F}(z)

(188)

we finally get the desired BPZ differential equation

\frac{z(1-z)}{b^{2}}\partial_{z}^{2}\mathcal{F}(z)+(2z-1)\partial_{z}\mathcal{F}(z)+\left(h_{\langle 2,1\rangle}+\frac{h_{1}}{z}-h_{3}+\frac{h_{2}}{1-z}\right)\mathcal{F}(z)=0.

(189)

There are
two
linearly independent solutions of (
189
) given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(\varepsilon=\pm)}(z)

\displaystyle=z^{\frac{bQ}{2}-\varepsilon bp_{1}}(1-z)^{\frac{bQ}{2}-bp_{2}}

\displaystyle\times{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}-bp_{2}+\varepsilon b(p_{3}-p_{1}),\frac{1}{2}-bp_{2}+\varepsilon b(-p_{3}-p_{1});1-2\varepsilon bp_{1};z\right).

(190)

These are the two conformal blocks propagating in the
s-
channel OPE (between
V_{p_{\langle 2,1\rangle}}V_{p_{1}}
and
V_{p_{3}}V_{p_{2}}
, see left of Fig.
8
). The internal Liouville momenta are
p_{s}=p_{1}+\frac{\epsilon b}{2}
and can be read from the exponent of the leading
z\rightarrow 0
power

\frac{bQ}{2}-\varepsilon bp_{1}=-\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4}-p_{1}^{2}\right)-\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4}-p_{\langle 2,1\rangle}^{2}\right)+\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4}-\left(p_{1}+\frac{\varepsilon b}{2}\right)^{2}\right).

(191)

The
t-
channel blocks
\mathcal{F}_{t}^{(\eta)}(z)
have internal momenta
p_{t}=p_{2}+\frac{\eta b}{2}\ (\eta=\pm
) (see right of Fig.
8
) and are given by the same expression as in (
B
) after exchanging
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}
and
z\leftrightarrow 1-z
, namely

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{t}^{(\eta=\pm)}(z)

\displaystyle=z^{\frac{bQ}{2}-bp_{1}}(1-z)^{\frac{bQ}{2}-\eta bp_{2}}

\displaystyle\times{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}-bp_{1}+\eta b(p_{3}-p_{2}),\frac{1}{2}-bp_{1}+\eta b(-p_{3}-p_{2});1-2\eta bp_{2};1-z\right).

(192)

As before, one reads the internal momenta from the exponent in the leading
z\rightarrow 1
power

\frac{bQ}{2}-\eta bp_{2}=-\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4}-p_{2}^{2}\right)-\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4}-p_{\langle 2,1\rangle}^{2}\right)+\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4}-\left(p_{2}+\frac{\eta b}{2}\right)^{2}\right).

(193)

p_{\langle 2,1\rangle}
p_{1}
p_{3}
p_{2}
~~~~~~~~=\ \sum_{\eta=\pm}\mathbb{F}_{\epsilon,\eta}
p_{1}+\frac{\varepsilon b}{2}
p_{1}
p_{3}
p_{2}
p_{\langle 2,1\rangle}
p_{2}+\frac{\eta b}{2}

Figure 8
:
Crossing transformation of sphere four-point conformal blocks with one insertion of a
\langle 2,1\rangle
degenerate operator. The fusion kernel is a
2\times 2
matrix given in (
197
).

The correlation function then admits as usual the
s,t
channel expansions (involving holomorphic and anti-holomorphic conformal blocks)

\mathcal{F}(z)=\sum_{\varepsilon=\pm 1}c_{\varepsilon}^{(s)}\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(\varepsilon)}(z)\overline{\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(\varepsilon)}(z)}=\sum_{\eta=\pm 1}c_{\eta}^{(t)}\mathcal{F}_{t}^{(\eta)}(z)\overline{\mathcal{F}_{t}^{(\eta)}(z)}

(194)

where

\displaystyle c_{\varepsilon}^{(s)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{C^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 2,1\rangle},p_{1}+\frac{\varepsilon b}{2})C^{(b)}(p_{1}+\frac{\varepsilon b}{2},p_{2},p_{3})}{B^{(b)}\left(p_{1}+\frac{\varepsilon b}{2}\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle c_{\eta}^{(t)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{C^{(b)}(p_{2},p_{\langle 2,1\rangle},p_{2}+\frac{\eta b}{2})C^{(b)}(p_{2}+\frac{\eta b}{2},p_{1},p_{3})}{B^{(b)}\left(p_{2}+\frac{\eta b}{2}\right)}.

(195)

Here
C^{(b)}(p_{i},p_{j},p_{k})
are the structure constants of the theory (that we are eventually interested in computing), and
B^{(b)}(p_{i})
is an arbitrary two-point function normalization.

Using the properties of the hypergeometric function
42
42

42

In particular, the identity

{}_{2}F_{1}\left(a,f,c,z\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(c)\Gamma(c-a-f)}{\Gamma(c-a)\Gamma(c-f)}\,_{2}F_{1}(a,f,a+f-c+1,1-z)

(196)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\Gamma(c)\Gamma(a+f-c)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(f)}(1-z)^{c-a-f}\,_{2}F_{1}(c-a,c-f,c-a-f+1,1-z).

it is straightforward to show that a
s-
channel conformal block can be written as a linear combination of
t-
channel blocks as

\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(\epsilon)}(z)=\sum_{\eta=\pm}\mathbb{F}_{\epsilon,\eta}\ \mathcal{F}_{t}^{(\eta)}(z)\ ,~~~~~~~\mathbb{F}_{\epsilon,\eta}:=\frac{\Gamma\left(1-2b\epsilon p_{1}\right)\Gamma\left(2b\eta p_{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+b\left(-\epsilon p_{1}+\eta p_{2}\pm p_{3}\right)\right)}\ ,\ \ \epsilon,\eta=\pm.

(197)

The coefficients
\mathbb{F}_{\epsilon,\eta}
define a
2\times 2

fusion kernel
. The form of this fusion kernel severely constraints the CFT data entering in the expansions (
194
). Indeed, crossing symmetry (
194
) combined with (
197
) imply the constraint

\sum_{\epsilon=\pm}\frac{C^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 2,1\rangle},p_{1}+\frac{\epsilon b}{2})C^{(b)}(p_{1}+\frac{\epsilon b}{2},p_{2},p_{3})}{B^{(b)}(p_{1}+\frac{\epsilon b}{2})}\mathbb{F}_{\epsilon,+}\mathbb{F}_{\epsilon,-}=0.

(198)

At this point it is desirable to eliminate the structure constants involving the degenerate field
p_{\langle 2,1\rangle}
. We can do this easily by setting
p_{2}=p_{\langle 2,1\rangle}
and
p_{3}=p_{1}
in (
198
), which then yields an equation that involves only (the squares of)
C^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 2,1\rangle},p_{1}\pm\frac{\epsilon b}{2})
. Using the latter back in (
198
) and after some rearrangement we get

\frac{C^{(b)}(p_{1}+\frac{b}{2},p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(b)}(p_{1}+\frac{b}{2})}{C^{(b)}(p_{1}-\frac{b}{2},p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(b)}(p_{1}-\frac{b}{2})}=\kappa_{b}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3}),

(199)

where

\displaystyle\kappa_{b}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3}):=-

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}\pm(b^{2}+\frac{1}{2})+2bp_{1}\right)\Gamma\left(1+2bp_{1}\right)^{2}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}\pm(b^{2}+\frac{1}{2})-2bp_{1}\right)\Gamma\left(1-2bp_{1}\right)^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+b(-p_{1}\pm p_{2}\pm p_{3})\right)^{2}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+b(p_{1}\pm p_{2}\pm p_{3})\right)^{2}}.

(200)

As in the main text, the
\pm
indicates a product over all possible combinations.
Equation (
199
) is a shift relation for the normalization-independent bootstrap quantity
(C^{(b)}_{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}})^{2}/B^{(b)}(p_{1})
, which we now interpret as the expansion coefficient of an ‘auxiliary’ four-point correlation function with pairwise identical external operators
p_{2},p_{3}
(
p_{1}
being the exchanged internal one). In other words, even though we started with the correlation function (
181
) involving the degenerate field
p_{\langle 2,1\rangle}
, we ended up with an equation that involves only the unspecified momenta
p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}
43
43

43

The information about the degenerate field is basically encoded in the function
\kappa_{b}
.
.

We emphasize that even though (
199
) is a shift relation in
p_{1}
, we could have equivalently derived identical equations involving shifts in either
p_{2},p_{3}
. At this point we claim that it is natural to search for a solution of the structure constants that is permutation-invariant under all exchanges of momenta. There is furthermore an identical shift relation after replacing
b\rightarrow b^{-1}
, since all the quantities are ultimately functions of the central charge
c
. The
b
and
b^{-1}
shift relations are incommensurable only in the case where
b^{2}\notin\mathbb{Q}
. In what follows, we will mainly focus on the shift relation (
199
), while keeping in mind that there is a counterpart involving shifts by
b^{-1}
.

We pause to track some properties of
\kappa_{b}
. It is permutation symmetric under exchanging
p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{3}
, but not with
p_{1}
. It is further invariant under reflections of either
p_{2}
or
p_{3}
, but
not
under reflections of
p_{1}
. It is only invariant under reflections of
p_{1}
when we perform a simultaneous reflection on
b
. In summary,

\kappa_{b}(p_{1}|p_{3},p_{2})=\kappa_{b}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3})\ ,\ \ \kappa_{-b}(-p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3})=\kappa_{b}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3})~.

(201)

Furthermore, under the VWR map
b\rightarrow ib
,
p_{j}\rightarrow ip_{j}
it satisfies an almost inverse relation with the original quantity:

\kappa_{b}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3})=\frac{\left(\frac{p_{1}+\frac{b}{2}}{p_{1}-\frac{b}{2}}\right)^{2}}{\kappa_{-ib}(-ip_{1}|-ip_{2},-ip_{3})}\ .

(202)

Just like in the main text for the
\mathcal{N}=1
case, this property will turn out to be crucial shortly when we discuss solutions of the shift relations in the timelike central charge regime
c\leqslant 1
.

Spacelike structure constants

Our main objective is to construct a solution of (
199
) for the structure constants
C^{(b)}
that is permutation symmetric under the exchange of
p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}
, reflection symmetric in each
p_{i}\rightarrow-p_{i}
, and invariant under
b\leftrightarrow b^{-1}
. With the additional assumption of meromorphicity in
p_{i}
’s and continuity in
b^{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, such a solution is essentially unique
Teschner:1995yf
44
44

44

When
b^{2}\in\mathbb{C}
the uniqueness argument fails and two given solutions can differ by a doubly-periodic function of the momenta with periods
b
and
b^{-1}

Zamolodchikov:2005fy
.
. The so-called ‘spacelike’ structure constants refer to solutions of (
199
) for
c\geqslant 25
. Therefore we want to solve

\frac{C^{(b)}(p_{1}+\frac{b}{2},p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(b)}(p_{1}+\frac{b}{2})}{C^{(b)}(p_{1}-\frac{b}{2},p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(b)}(p_{1}-\frac{b}{2})}=\kappa_{b}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3})\ ,\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{for}\ \ b\in\mathbb{R}_{(0,1]}\ .

(203)

Starting from (
203
) we can make several convenient choices for the two-point structure constant
B^{(b)}
which will then lead to different solutions for the structure constants with the aforementioned properties. We record two such choices that are of particular interest in the literature.

DOZZ normalization.

The most common normalization of the two- and three-point structure constants is inspired by the action of spacelike Liouville theory (see e.g. eqns (2.2),(2.6) in
Zamolodchikov:2005fy
). It reads

B^{(b)}_{\text{DOZZ}}(p)=\lambda^{2b^{-1}p}\frac{\gamma(1-2bp)}{b^{2}\gamma(1+2b^{-1}p)}

(204)

where
\lambda\equiv\pi\mu\gamma(b^{2})
and
\mu
the usual cosmological constant that enters in the Lagrangian of the theory. With this two-point function, the structure constants that solve (
203
) read

C^{(b)}_{\text{DOZZ}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})=(\lambda b^{2-2b^{2}})^{b^{-1}(-\frac{Q}{2}+p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})}\Upsilon_{0}\frac{\Gamma_{b}(\frac{Q}{2}\pm p_{1}\pm p_{2}\pm p_{3})}{\prod_{j=1}^{3}\Gamma_{b}(Q-2p_{j})\Gamma_{b}(2p_{j})}

(205)

where
\Upsilon_{0}\equiv\text{Res}_{x=0}[\partial_{x}\Upsilon_{b}(x)]
is a convenient constant (which doesn’t affect the shift relations).

Natural normalization.

The following normalization was recently used in
Collier:2023cyw
(see also
Collier:2019weq
;
Eberhardt:2023mrq
). In this normalization we identify the two-point function with the inverse of the modular kernel of the identity torus character in 2d CFTs. Indeed, it is known that

\chi_{\mathds{1}}(-1/\tau)=\int_{i\mathbb{R}}\frac{\text{d}p}{2}\rho^{(b)}_{0}(p)\chi_{p}(\tau)\ ,

(206)

with

\rho_{0}^{(b)}(p)=-4\sqrt{2}\sin{(2\pi bp)}\sin{(2\pi b^{-1}p)}~,\quad\chi_{p}(\tau)=\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-2\pi i\tau p^{2}}}{\eta(\tau)}~.

(207)

We then make the choice

B_{\text{VMS}}^{(b)}(p)=\frac{1}{\rho_{0}^{(b)}(p)}~.

(208)

This causes an essential simplification in (
203
) since known quantities form a total square. It is straightforward to see that the equation takes the form

\frac{C_{\text{VMS}}^{(b)}(p_{1}-\frac{b}{2},p_{2},p_{3})}{C_{\text{VMS}}^{(b)}(p_{1}+\frac{b}{2},p_{2},p_{3})}=\frac{\Gamma\left(1-2bp_{1}\right)\Gamma\left(1+b^{2}-2bp_{1}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+b(p_{1}\pm p_{2}\pm p_{3})\right)}{\Gamma\left(1+2bp_{1}\right)\Gamma\left(1+b^{2}+2bp_{1}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+b(-p_{1}\pm p_{2}\pm p_{3})\right)}\ .

(209)

A unique solution to (
209
) that is meromorphic in the momenta, continuous in
b\in(0,1]
, symmetric under reflections
p_{i}\rightarrow-p_{i}
, symmetric under any permutation of
p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}
, invariant under
b\leftrightarrow b^{-1}
, and reproduces the two-point structure constant normalization as

\lim_{p_{3}\rightarrow Q/2}C^{(b)}_{\text{VMS}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})=\frac{1}{\rho^{(b)}_{0}(p_{1})}(\delta(p_{1}-p_{2})+\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}))\ ,

(210)

is given by

C^{(b)}_{\text{VMS}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})=\frac{\Gamma_{b}(2Q)\Gamma_{b}(\frac{Q}{2}\pm p_{1}\pm p_{2}\pm p_{3})}{\sqrt{2}\Gamma_{b}(Q)^{3}\prod_{j=1}^{3}\Gamma_{b}(Q\pm 2p_{j})}.

(211)

Timelike structure constants

Just as we did for the
\mathcal{N}=1
case in the main text, we will describe the derivation of the timelike structure constants using the symmetry of the shift relations (
199
) under VWR.

For
b\in(0,1]
(or
c\geqslant 25
) we have already found the solutions (
208
), (
211
), i.e.

\frac{C^{(b)}(p_{1}+\frac{b}{2},p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(b)}(p_{1}+\frac{b}{2})}{C^{(b)}(p_{1}-\frac{b}{2},p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(b)}(p_{1}-\frac{b}{2})}=\kappa_{b}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3})\ ,\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{for}\ \ c\geqslant 25~.

(212)

For
b=-i\hat{b}
, with
\hat{b}\in\mathbb{R}_{(0,1]}
(or
c\leqslant 1
), let us denote the corresponding (and still unknown) solutions as
\widehat{B}^{(\hat{b})},\widehat{C}^{(\hat{b})}
. They obviously satisfy the same shift relation in the corresponding central charge regime

\frac{\widehat{C}^{(\hat{b})}(p_{1}+\frac{(-i\hat{b})}{2},p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/\widehat{B}^{(\hat{b})}(p_{1}+\frac{(-i\hat{b})}{2})}{\widehat{C}^{(\hat{b})}(p_{1}-\frac{(-i\hat{b})}{2},p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/\widehat{B}^{(\hat{b})}(p_{1}-\frac{(-i\hat{b})}{2})}=\kappa_{-i\hat{b}}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3})\ ,\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{for}\ \ \ \ c\leqslant 1~.

(213)

We can now pin down the new structure constants
\widehat{B}^{(\hat{b})},\widehat{C}^{(\hat{b})}
by implementing the VWR of the shift equations. Indeed, our strategy is to start from (
212
) and, by leveraging the analytic properties of

\kappa_{b}
(in particular using (
202
)), gradually construct a solution to (
213
).

Relabelling
b\equiv\hat{b}\in\mathbb{R}
in (
212
) and using (
202
) yields

\frac{C^{(\hat{b})}(p_{1}+\frac{\hat{b}}{2},p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(\hat{b})}(p_{1}+\frac{\hat{b}}{2})}{C^{(\hat{b})}(p_{1}-\frac{\hat{b}}{2},p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(\hat{b})}(p_{1}-\frac{\hat{b}}{2})}=\kappa_{\hat{b}}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3})=\frac{\left(\frac{p_{1}+\frac{\hat{b}}{2}}{p_{1}-\frac{\hat{b}}{2}}\right)^{2}}{\kappa_{-i\hat{b}}(-ip_{1}|-ip_{2},-ip_{3})}~.

(214)

Next, let’s re-name
p_{k}\rightarrow ip_{k}
. This gives

\displaystyle\frac{C^{(\hat{b})}(i(p_{1}+\frac{(-i\hat{b})}{2}),ip_{2},ip_{3})^{2}/B^{(\hat{b})}(i(p_{1}+\frac{(-i\hat{b})}{2}))}{C^{(\hat{b})}(i(p_{1}-\frac{(-i\hat{b})}{2}),ip_{2},ip_{3})^{2}/B^{(\hat{b})}(i(p_{1}-\frac{(-i\hat{b})}{2}))}=\frac{\left(\frac{p_{1}-\frac{i\hat{b}}{2}}{p_{1}+\frac{i\hat{b}}{2}}\right)^{2}}{\kappa_{-i\hat{b}}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3})}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\ \ \left(\frac{p_{1}-\frac{i\hat{b}}{2}}{p_{1}+\frac{i\hat{b}}{2}}\right)^{2}\frac{C^{(\hat{b})}(i(p_{1}-\frac{(-i\hat{b})}{2}),ip_{2},ip_{3})^{2}/B^{(\hat{b})}(i(p_{1}-\frac{(-i\hat{b})}{2}))}{C^{(\hat{b})}(i(p_{1}+\frac{(-i\hat{b})}{2}),ip_{2},ip_{3})^{2}/B^{(\hat{b})}(i(p_{1}+\frac{(-i\hat{b})}{2}))}=\kappa_{-i\hat{b}}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3})

(215)

The last equation has exactly the form (
213
) which we wanted to solve.

There is of course some freedom on how to identify
\widehat{B}^{(\hat{b})},\widehat{C}^{(\hat{b})}
in (
B
). The most natural choice is the one where the three-point structure constant is just the
inverse
of the VWR-ed structure constant (
211
). We denote this particular choice as
\widehat{C}^{(\hat{b})}_{\text{VMS}}
. We then get

\widehat{C}^{(\hat{b})}_{\text{VMS}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})\equiv\frac{1}{C_{\text{VMS}}^{(\hat{b})}(ip_{1},ip_{2},ip_{3})}=\frac{\sqrt{2}\Gamma_{\hat{b}}(\hat{b}+\hat{b}^{-1})^{3}\prod_{j=1}^{3}\Gamma_{\hat{b}}(\hat{b}+\hat{b}^{-1}\pm 2ip_{j})}{\Gamma_{\hat{b}}(2\hat{b}+2\hat{b}^{-1})\Gamma_{\hat{b}}(\frac{\hat{b}+\hat{b}^{-1}}{2}\pm ip_{1}\pm ip_{2}\pm ip_{3})}.

(216)

This then leaves no room for the choice of two-point function (modulo momentum-independent factors), namely

\widehat{B}^{(\hat{b})}_{\text{VMS}}(p)\equiv\frac{1}{p^{2}B^{(\hat{b})}_{\text{VMS}}(ip)}=\frac{\rho^{{(\hat{b})}}_{0}(ip)}{p^{2}}.

(217)

Zamolodchikov’s normalization.

The timelike structure constants of
\mathcal{N}=0
Liouville theory were originally derived in
Zamolodchikov:2005fy
, where the following normalization was adopted
45
45

45

To connect with e.g. (5.1) of the paper we should take
\alpha_{k}=\frac{\hat{b}^{-1}-\hat{b}}{2}-ip_{k}
.

\begin{aligned} \widehat{B}^{(\hat{b})}_{\text{Z}}(p)&=1\ ,\\
\widehat{C}^{(\hat{b})}_{\text{Z}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})&=A\frac{\left[\prod_{j=1}^{3}\Gamma_{\hat{b}}\left(\pm 2ip_{j}+\hat{b}\right)\Gamma_{\hat{b}}\left(\pm 2ip_{j}+\hat{b}^{-1}\right)\right]^{1/2}}{\Gamma_{\hat{b}}\left(\frac{\hat{b}+\hat{b}^{-1}}{2}\pm ip_{1}\pm ip_{2}\pm ip_{3}\right)}\end{aligned}.

(218)

Here
A\equiv\frac{\hat{b}^{\hat{b}^{-2}-\hat{b}^{2}-1}[\gamma(\hat{b}^{2})\gamma(\hat{b}^{-2}-1)]^{1/2}}{\Upsilon_{\hat{b}}(\hat{b})}
and is chosen such that
\widehat{C}^{(\hat{b})}_{\text{Z}}(p,p,i(\hat{b}^{-1}-\hat{b})/2)=1
. One can verify that these expressions satisfy (
213
). Despite the bizarre-looking square-root, this particular normalization of the three-point structure constants can be shown to match the structure constants of the Virasoro minimal models
Zamolodchikov:2005fy
when evaluated at the appropriate values of the central charge and conformal dimensions.

Another important and different normalization of the timelike structure constants was discussed in
Harlow:2011ny
, where their particular choice originated from the interpretation of the timelike Liouville
path integral as being a different integration cycle of ordinary Liouville theory.

Appendix C
Derivation of the shift relations in the NS-sector

In this appendix we revisit the derivation of the shift relations for the NS-sector structure constants. Our approach relies on the NS-sector null vector (
15
) and is conceptually analogous to the non-supersymmetric case which we revisited in appendix
B
, i.e. it involves no superspace formalism. The only difference will be that instead of a second order differential equation, inserting the degenerate field
V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}^{\mathrm{NS}}
into a four point function leads to a third order differential equation. For notational convenience we omit the NS superscript. All the fields in this appendix are in the NS-sector.

Our approach toward the null vector differential equations follows the logic in
Belavin:2007eq
;
Belavin:2007gz
(see also
Ivanova:2025pkv
).
We will derive two differential equations for the four-point functions

\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle\ ,\quad\langle\Lambda_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle~

(219)

with the degenerate fields
V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}
and
\Lambda_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}=G_{-1/2}V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}
, with
p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}=\frac{1}{2b}+\frac{3b}{2}
,
p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\in i\mathbb{R}
.
We then combine these two equations to obtain a third order differential equation for
\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle
.

1st equation.

We start by acting on (
15
) with
G_{-1/2}
from the left and using the algebra (
7
) we obtain

\left[\frac{1}{b^{2}}L_{-1}^{2}+2L_{-2}\right]V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}=G_{-3/2}\Lambda_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}~.

(220)

The left hand side now looks similar to the non-supersymmetric null vector equation (
182
). Borrowing the result (
B
) we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg\{\prod_{i=1}^{3}(z_{0}-z_{i})\left(-\frac{1}{2b^{2}}\partial_{z_{0}}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{\partial_{z_{0}}}{z_{0}-z_{i}}\right)+(3z_{0}-z_{1}-z_{2}-z_{3})h_{\langle 1,3\rangle}

\displaystyle+\frac{z_{12}z_{13}}{z_{1}-z_{0}}h_{1}+\frac{z_{21}z_{23}}{z_{2}-z_{0}}h_{2}+\frac{z_{31}z_{32}}{z_{3}-z_{0}}h_{3}\bigg\}\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle

(221)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\prod_{i=1}^{3}(z_{0}-z_{i})\langle G_{-3/2}\Lambda_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{1}(z_{1})V_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle\ .

(222)

For the right hand side we use

\oint_{\infty}\text{d}z\ \varepsilon(z)\langle G(z)\Lambda_{p_{\langle 3,1\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle=0~.

(223)

We now choose
\varepsilon(z)=(z-z_{0})^{-1}
and also use the fact that
G(z)=\mathcal{O}(z^{-3})
as
z\rightarrow\infty
46
46

46

Under the conformal transformation
z\rightarrow\omega=1/z
the supercurrent transforms as

G^{\prime}(\omega)=\left(\frac{\text{d}\omega}{\text{d}z}\right)^{-\frac{3}{2}}G(z)=z^{3}G(z)~,

(224)

where we used
h=\tilde{h}=\frac{3}{2}
.
Regularity then implies that
G(z)\sim\mathcal{O}(z^{-3})
as
z\rightarrow\infty
.

. Deforming the contour to include the points
z_{i}
,
i=\{0,1,2,3\}
(which we denote collectively by
\boldsymbol{z}
) we obtain

\langle G_{-3/2}\Lambda_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{1}(z_{1})V_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle=-\frac{f_{01}(\boldsymbol{z})}{(z_{0}-z_{1})}-\frac{f_{02}(\boldsymbol{z})}{(z_{0}-z_{2})}-\frac{f_{03}(\boldsymbol{z})}{(z_{0}-z_{3})}~,

(225)

where the subscript indicates the
z_{i}
locations of
\Lambda_{p}
operators, e.g.

\displaystyle f_{01}(\boldsymbol{z})\equiv\langle\Lambda_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})\Lambda_{p_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle~,

(226)

Finally we consider

\oint_{\infty}\text{d}z\ \varepsilon(z)\langle G(z)\Lambda_{p_{\langle 3,1\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle=0~,

(227)

with
\varepsilon(z)=1
. Performing the by now standard contour deformation we obtain

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\partial_{z_{0}}\langle V_{p_{\langle 3,1\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle-f_{01}(\boldsymbol{z})-f_{02}(\boldsymbol{z})-f_{03}(\boldsymbol{z})~.

(228)

We can eliminate for example
f_{01}
in favour of
f_{02}
and
f_{03}
, leading to

\displaystyle\bigg\{\prod_{i=1}^{3}(z_{0}-z_{i})\left(-\frac{1}{2b^{2}}\partial_{z_{0}}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{(1-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{i,1})\partial_{z_{0}}}{z_{0}-z_{i}}\right)+(3z_{0}-z_{1}-z_{2}-z_{3})h_{\langle 1,3\rangle}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{z_{12}z_{13}}{z_{1}-z_{0}}h_{1}+\frac{z_{21}z_{23}}{z_{2}-z_{0}}h_{2}+\frac{z_{31}z_{32}}{z_{3}-z_{0}}h_{3}\bigg\}\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle

(229)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}(z_{0}-z_{3})(z_{1}-z_{2})(f_{02}(\boldsymbol{z})+f_{03}(\boldsymbol{z}))~.

(230)

Expressing the four point functions in terms of the cross ratio
z
we get

\displaystyle\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle=\left[\prod_{i<j}(z_{i}-z_{j})^{\mu_{ij}}\right]\mathcal{F}(z)~,

(231)

\displaystyle f_{02}(\boldsymbol{z})=\left[\prod_{i<j}(z_{i}-z_{j})^{\nu_{ij}}\right]\mathcal{F}_{02}(z)~,\quad f_{03}(\boldsymbol{z})=\left[\prod_{i<j}(z_{i}-z_{j})^{\hat{\nu}_{ij}}\right]\mathcal{F}_{03}(z)~,

(232)

where
\mu_{ij}
is again given by

\mu_{ij}=\frac{1}{3}\sum_{k=0}^{3}h_{k}-h_{i}-h_{j},

(233)

and the
\nu_{ij}
’s are defined completely analogously with the only difference that in
h_{0}
and
h_{2}
we account for the
1/2
shift (or
h_{0}
and
h_{3}
respectively) since these are the conformal dimensions of the superdescendant fields
\Lambda_{p}=G_{-1/2}V_{p}
.
The derivatives with respect to
z_{0}
can furthermore be expressed in terms of the cross ratio
z
as

\displaystyle\partial_{z_{0}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu_{01}}{z}+\frac{\mu_{02}}{(z-1)}+\partial_{z}~,

\displaystyle\partial_{z_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu_{01}(\mu_{01}-1)}{z^{2}}+\frac{\mu_{02}(\mu_{02}-1)}{(z-1)^{2}}+\frac{2\mu_{01}\mu_{02}}{z(z-1)}+2\left(\frac{\mu_{01}}{z}+\frac{\mu_{02}}{(z-1)}\right)\partial_{z}+\partial_{z}^{2}~.

(234)

Taking the limits

z_{0}\rightarrow z~,\quad z_{1}\rightarrow 0~,\quad z_{2}\rightarrow 1~,\quad z_{3}\rightarrow\infty

(235)

we finally obtain

\displaystyle(-1)^{5/6}z^{1/6}(1-z)^{2/3}\bigg[\frac{1}{b^{2}}\partial_{z}^{2}+\left(2b^{-2}\left(\frac{\mu_{01}}{z}+\frac{\mu_{02}}{(z-1)}\right)+2\frac{1-2z}{z(z-1)}+\frac{1}{z}\right)\partial_{z}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{b^{2}}\left(\frac{\mu_{01}(\mu_{01}-1)}{z^{2}}+\frac{\mu_{02}(\mu_{02}-1)}{(z-1)^{2}}+\frac{2\mu_{01}\mu_{02}}{z(z-1)}\right)+2\frac{h_{1}-\mu_{01}}{z^{2}}

\displaystyle+2\frac{h_{2}-\mu_{02}}{(z-1)^{2}}+2\frac{\mu_{12}}{z(z-1)}+\frac{\mu_{01}}{z^{2}}+\frac{\mu_{02}}{z(z-1)}\bigg]\mathcal{F}(z)=-\frac{1}{z(z-1)}\mathcal{F}_{02}(z)~.

(236)

Performing the shift

\mathcal{F}(z)\rightarrow z^{-\mu_{01}}(1-z)^{-\mu_{02}}\mathcal{F}(z)~,\quad\mathcal{F}_{02}(z)\rightarrow z^{-\nu_{01}}(1-z)^{-\nu_{02}}\mathcal{F}_{02}(z)~,

(237)

this leads to our first desired equation

\left(\frac{1}{b^{2}}\partial_{z}^{2}+\frac{1-3z}{z(z-1)}\partial_{z}+\frac{2h_{1}}{z^{2}}+\frac{2h_{2}}{(z-1)^{2}}-2\frac{h_{0}+h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}}{z(z-1)}\right)\mathcal{F}(z)\cr=-\frac{1}{z(z-1)}\mathcal{F}_{02}(z)~.

(238)

2nd equation.

To obtain a second equation we take
\varepsilon(z)=(z-z_{0})^{-1}
and consider

0=\oint_{\infty}\text{d}z\,\varepsilon(z)\langle G(z)V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{1}(z_{1})\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle~.

(239)

Deforming the contour we obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\oint_{z_{0}}\text{d}z\,\varepsilon(z)\langle[G(z)V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})]_{\mathrm{OPE}}V_{1}(z_{1})\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle

(240)

\displaystyle\quad+\oint_{z_{1}}\text{d}z\,\varepsilon(z)\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})[G(z)V_{1}(z_{1})]_{\mathrm{OPE}}\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle

(241)

\displaystyle\quad+\oint_{z_{2}}\text{d}z\,\varepsilon(z)\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})[G(z)V_{1}(z_{1})[G(z)\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})]_{\mathrm{OPE}}V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle~

(242)

\displaystyle\quad-\oint_{z_{3}}\text{d}z\,\varepsilon(z)\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{1}(z_{1})\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})[G(z)V_{3}(z_{3})]_{\mathrm{OPE}}\rangle~.

(243)

In the first contour integral around
z_{0}
we expand
G(z)
to order
(z-z_{0})^{0}
, picking up a term
G_{-3/2}V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}
which, using the null vector (
15
), we replace by

G_{-3/2}V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}=-\frac{1}{b^{2}}G_{-1/2}^{3}V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}=-\frac{1}{b^{2}}L_{-1}G_{-1/2}V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}=-\frac{1}{b^{2}}\partial_{z_{0}}\Lambda_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})~.

(244)

We thus obtain

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{b^{2}}\partial_{z_{0}}\langle\Lambda_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{1}(z_{1})\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle-\frac{1}{z_{0}-z_{1}}\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})\Lambda_{1}(z_{1})\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle

(245)

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{2h_{2}}{(z_{0}-z_{2})^{2}}+\frac{1}{z_{0}-z_{2}}\partial_{z_{2}}\right)\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{1}(z_{1})V_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle

(246)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{z_{0}-z_{3}}\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{1}(z_{1})\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})\Lambda_{3}(z_{3})\rangle.

(247)

We can use

0=\oint_{\infty}\text{d}z\langle G(z)V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{1}(z_{1})\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle~,

(248)

which implies

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\langle\Lambda_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{1}(z_{1})\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle+\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})\Lambda_{1}(z_{1})\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle

(249)

\displaystyle\quad+\partial_{z_{2}}\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{1}(z_{1})V_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle-\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{1}(z_{1})\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})\Lambda_{3}(z_{3})\rangle,

(250)

in order to eliminate
\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})\Lambda_{1}(z_{1})\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle
in (
247
). We then get

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{b^{2}}\partial_{z_{0}}f_{02}(\boldsymbol{z})-\frac{1}{(z_{0}-z_{1})}f_{02}(\boldsymbol{z})-\left(\frac{1}{z_{0}-z_{1}}-\frac{1}{z_{0}-z_{2}}\right)\partial_{z_{2}}\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{1}(z_{1})\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle

(251)

\displaystyle+\frac{2h_{2}}{(z_{0}-z_{2})^{2}}\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}(z_{0})V_{1}(z_{1})\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle+\left(\frac{1}{z_{0}-z_{1}}-\frac{1}{z_{0}-z_{3}}\right)f_{03}(\boldsymbol{z})~.

(252)

Taking the limits (
235
) and using (
232
), we obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=(-1)^{5/6}z^{1/6}(1-z)^{2/3}\left(\frac{1}{z(z-1)}\left(-\frac{\mu_{02}}{z-1}+\mu_{12}-z\partial_{z}\right)\mathcal{F}(z)+\frac{2h_{2}}{(z-1)^{2}}\mathcal{F}(z)\right)

(253)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{b^{2}}\left(\frac{\nu_{01}}{z}+\frac{\nu_{02}}{z-1}+\partial_{z}\right)\mathcal{F}_{02}(z)-\frac{1}{z}\mathcal{F}_{02}(z)\right)~.

(254)

Finally, performing the shift

\mathcal{F}(z)\rightarrow z^{-\mu_{01}}(1-z)^{-\mu_{02}}\mathcal{F}(z)~,\quad\mathcal{F}_{02}(z)\rightarrow z^{-\nu_{01}}(1-z)^{-\nu_{02}}\mathcal{F}_{02}(z)~,

(255)

we land on our second equation

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{b^{2}}\partial_{z}-\frac{1}{z}\right)\mathcal{F}_{02}(z)+\left(\frac{1}{1-z}\partial_{z}+\frac{2h_{2}}{(z-1)^{2}}-\frac{h_{0}+h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}}{z(z-1)}\right)\mathcal{F}(z)=0~.

(256)

Combined differential equation.

Combining (
238
) and (
256
) and using that the operator
V_{\langle 1,3\rangle}
has conformal dimension
h_{\langle 1,3\rangle}=-1/2-b^{2}
we obtain the differential equation
Belavin:2007gz
;
Belavin:2007eq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{b^{2}}\mathcal{F}{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\frac{1-2b^{2}}{b^{2}}\frac{1-2z}{z(1-z)}\mathcal{F}{{}^{\prime\prime}}+\left(\frac{b^{2}+2h_{1}}{z^{2}}+\frac{b^{2}+2h_{2}}{(1-z)^{2}}+\frac{2-3b^{2}+2h_{1+2-3}}{z(1-z)}\right)\mathcal{F}{{}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2h_{2}(1+b^{2})}{(1-z)^{3}}-\frac{2h_{1}(1+b^{2})}{z^{3}}+\frac{h_{2-1}+(1-2z)(b^{4}+b^{2}(1/2-h_{1+2-3})-h_{1+2})}{z^{2}(1-z)^{2}}\right)\mathcal{F}=0.

(257)

Similar equation holds for
\bar{z}
.

The shift relations.

There are now
three
linearly independent solutions of (
C
) given by

47

47

Note that, compared to

\alpha_{k}=\frac{Q}{2}-p_{k}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(+)}(z)

\displaystyle=z^{\frac{bQ}{2}-bp_{1}}(1-z)^{\frac{bQ}{2}-bp_{2}}\times\mathcal{L}_{(+)}^{2}\times\mathcal{I}_{1}\left(z\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(0)}(z)

\displaystyle=z^{\frac{bQ}{2}-bp_{1}}(1-z)^{\frac{bQ}{2}-bp_{2}}\times\mathcal{L}_{(0)}^{2}\times\mathcal{I}_{2}\left(z\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(-)}(z)

\displaystyle=z^{\frac{bQ}{2}-bp_{1}}(1-z)^{\frac{bQ}{2}-bp_{2}}\times\mathcal{L}_{(-)}^{2}\times\mathcal{I}_{3}\left(z\right),

(258)

where the momentum-dependent leg factors read

\displaystyle\textstyle\mathcal{L}_{(\varepsilon=\pm)}\equiv\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{-1-b^{2}}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(1-\varepsilon bp_{1}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1-b^{2}}{2}-\varepsilon bp_{1}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{3+b^{2}}{4}-\frac{b}{2}(p_{2}+\varepsilon(p_{1}-p_{3}))\right)^{-1}\Gamma\left(\frac{1-b^{2}}{4}-\frac{b}{2}(p_{2}+\varepsilon(p_{1}-p_{3}))\right)^{-1}}{\Gamma\left(-1-b^{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{3+b^{2}}{4}+\frac{b}{2}(p_{2}-\varepsilon(p_{1}+p_{3}))\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1-b^{2}}{4}+\frac{b}{2}(p_{2}-\varepsilon(p_{1}+p_{3}))\right)},

(259)

\displaystyle\textstyle\mathcal{L}_{(0)}\equiv\frac{b^{2}\Gamma\left(\frac{1+b^{2}}{2}+bp_{1}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1+b^{2}}{2}-bp_{1}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{3+b^{2}}{4}+\frac{b}{2}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{3+b^{2}}{4}+\frac{b}{2}(p_{1}-p_{2}-p_{3})\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{3+b^{2}}{4}+\frac{b}{2}(-p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3})\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{3+b^{2}}{4}+\frac{b}{2}(-p_{1}-p_{2}+p_{3})\right)}~.

(260)

The special functions
\mathcal{I}(z)
depend additionally on the momenta
p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}
as well as the central charge
c
, and admit integral representations of Dotsenko-Fateev type. Their properties are explained in detail in
Belavin:2007gz
and we will not repeat them here
48
48

48

To avoid confusion, we actually adopt the same notation as in eqn. (C.7) in Appendix C of
Belavin:2007gz
.
. What is important for us is their small
z
expansions

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}_{(+)}}(1+\cdots),

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}_{(0)}}z^{bp_{1}+\frac{1+b^{2}}{2}}(1+\cdots),

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}_{(-)}}z^{2bp_{1}}(1+\cdots).

(261)

The functions (
C
) are the three
s-
channel conformal blocks in the OPE between
V_{p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}}V_{p_{1}}
and
V_{p_{3}}V_{p_{2}}
(see left of Fig.
9
). The internal Liouville momenta are now
p_{s}=p_{1}+\varepsilon b
with
\varepsilon=\pm
, and
h_{s}=h_{1}+1/2
. They can be read consistently from the corresponding exponents of the leading
z\rightarrow 0
powers of those functions, namely from
\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(\pm)}
we get

\displaystyle\frac{bQ}{2}+\varepsilon bp_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4}-p_{1}^{2}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4}-p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4}-\left(p_{1}-\varepsilon b\right)^{2}\right),

(262)

and from
\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(0)}
,

1+b^{2}=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4}-p_{1}^{2}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4}-p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4}-p_{1}^{2}+1\right)~.

(263)

The
t-
channel blocks
\mathcal{F}_{t}^{(\eta=\pm,0)}(z)
are obtained by the same expressions as in (
C
) after exchanging
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}
and
z\leftrightarrow 1-z
, namely

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{t}^{(+)}(z)

\displaystyle=z^{\frac{bQ}{2}-bp_{1}}(1-z)^{\frac{bQ}{2}-bp_{2}}\times(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{(+)})^{2}\times\mathcal{J}_{1}\left(z\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{t}^{(0)}(z)

\displaystyle=z^{\frac{bQ}{2}-bp_{1}}(1-z)^{\frac{bQ}{2}-bp_{2}}\times(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{(0)})^{2}\times\mathcal{J}_{2}\left(z\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{t}^{(-)}(z)

\displaystyle=z^{\frac{bQ}{2}-bp_{1}}(1-z)^{\frac{bQ}{2}-bp_{2}}\times(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{(-)})^{2}\times\mathcal{J}_{3}\left(z\right),

(264)

where we denoted as
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
the leg factors given in (
259
) after exchanging
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}
, and
\mathcal{J}_{i}(z)
are the special functions that result from
\mathcal{I}_{i}(z)
accordingly. Their expansions near
z\rightarrow 1
are

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{(+)}}(1+\cdots),

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{(0)}}(1-z)^{bp_{2}+\frac{1+b^{2}}{2}}(1+\cdots),

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{3}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{(-)}}(1-z)^{2bp_{2}}(1+\cdots).

(265)

Combining the holomorphic and the anti-holomorphic sectors, the four-point function admits the
s,t
channel expansions

\displaystyle z^{bp_{1}-\frac{bQ}{2}}(1-z)^{bp_{2}-\frac{bQ}{2}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{3}c_{i}^{(s)}\mathcal{I}_{i}(z)\overline{\mathcal{I}_{i}}(z)=\sum_{i=1}^{3}c_{i}^{(t)}\mathcal{J}_{i}(z)\overline{\mathcal{J}_{i}}(z)

(266)

where

\displaystyle c_{1}^{(s)}:=\mathcal{L}_{(+)}^{2}\frac{C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{1}+b)C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1}+b,p_{2},p_{3})}{B_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}\left(p_{1}+b\right)},

\displaystyle c_{2}^{(s)}:=-\mathcal{L}_{(0)}^{2}\frac{\tilde{C}_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{1})\tilde{C}_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})}{B_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}\left(p_{1}\right)},

\displaystyle c_{3}^{(s)}:=\mathcal{L}_{(-)}^{2}\frac{C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{1}-b)C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1}-b,p_{2},p_{3})}{B_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}\left(p_{1}-b\right)},

and analogously for
c^{(t)}_{i}
. Here
C^{(b)}_{\text{NS}},\tilde{C}_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}
are the NS structure constants of the theory that we are eventually interested in computing, and
B_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}
is an arbitrary two-point function normalization.

There is now a non-trivial
fusion kernel
that expresses a given special function
\mathcal{I}_{i}
as a linear combination of
\mathcal{J}_{i}
’s.
In appendix C of
Belavin:2007gz
, the authors wrote down explicitly the matrix that implements this non-trivial relation. We get

\mathcal{I}_{k}(z)=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{k,j}\ \mathcal{J}_{j}(z).

(268)

where the entries of this
3\times 3
fusion matrix read

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{s\left(b^{2}-1+2bp_{1-2-3}\right)s\left(-3-b^{2}+2bp_{1-2-3}\right)}{s\left(-2+2b^{2}+4bp_{2}\right)s\left(4(bp_{2}-1)\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{12}

\displaystyle=\frac{s\left(b^{2}-1+2bp_{1-2-3}\right)s\left(b^{2}-1+2bp_{1+2+3}\right)}{s\left(-2+2b^{2}+4bp_{2}\right)s\left(2b^{2}-4bp_{2}+6\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{13}

\displaystyle=\frac{s\left(b^{2}-1+2bp_{1+2+3}\right)s\left(-3-b^{2}+2bp_{1+2+3}\right)}{s\left(4(bp_{2}-1)\right)s\left(-2b^{2}+4bp_{2}-6\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{21}

\displaystyle=\frac{s\left(-3-b^{2}+2bp_{1-2-3}\right)s\left(b^{2}-5+2bp_{1+2-3}\right)}{(2s\left(2b^{2}\right))^{-1}s\left(-2+2b^{2}+4bp_{2}\right)s\left(4(bp_{2}-1)\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{22}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\left(\cos(\pi bp_{1})\cos(\pi bp_{2})-s\left(2b^{2}\right)\cos(\pi bp_{3})\right)}{\cos\left(\pi b^{2}\right)+\cos(2\pi bp_{2})}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{23}

\displaystyle=\frac{s\left(-3-b^{2}+2bp_{1+2+3}\right)s\left(3+b^{2}+2bp_{1-2+3}\right)}{(2s\left(2b^{2}\right))^{-1}s\left(4(bp_{2}-1)\right)s\left(-2b^{2}+4bp_{2}-6\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{31}

\displaystyle=\frac{s\left(b^{2}-5+2bp_{1+2-3}\right)s\left(-7-b^{2}+2bp_{1+2-3}\right)}{s\left(-2+2b^{2}+4bp_{2}\right)s\left(4(bp_{2}-1)\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{32}

\displaystyle=\frac{s\left(b^{2}-1+2bp_{2-1-3}\right)s\left(-7-b^{2}+2bp_{1+2-3}\right)}{s\left(-2+2b^{2}+4bp_{2}\right)s\left(-2b^{2}+4bp_{2}-6\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{33}

\displaystyle=\frac{s\left(b^{2}-1+2bp_{2-1-3}\right)s\left(-3-b^{2}+2bp_{2-1-3}\right)}{s\left(4(bp_{2}-1)\right)s\left(-2b^{2}+4bp_{2}-6\right)}~.

(269)

Here
s(x)\equiv\sin{(\pi x/4)}
, and we also use the notation
p_{1\pm 2\pm 3}=p_{1}\pm p_{2}\pm p_{3}
for brevity.

p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}
p_{1}
p_{3}
p_{2}
~~~~~~~~=\ \sum\limits_{j\in\{+,0,-\}}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{k,j}
p_{1}+kb
p_{1}

Figure 9
:
Crossing transformation of sphere four-point conformal blocks with one insertion of a
\langle 1,3\rangle
degenerate NS-sector operator. The fusion kernel is a
3\times 3
matrix given in (

).

The form of this fusion kernel severely constraints the CFT data in the NS-sector of
\mathcal{N}=1
Liouville. Indeed, crossing symmetry (
C
) combined with (
268
) imply the following constraints which we express in matrix form:

M\cdot\vec{c}=0

(270)

where

M=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{11}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{12}&~~\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{21}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{22}&~~\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{31}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{32}\\
\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{11}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{13}&~~\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{21}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{23}&~~\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{31}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{33}\\
\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{12}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{13}&~~\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{22}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{23}&~~\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{32}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{33}\end{pmatrix}~~,~~\vec{c}=\begin{pmatrix}c^{(s)}_{1}\\
c^{(s)}_{2}\\
c^{(s)}_{3}\end{pmatrix}.

(271)

We will now establish that there are
two independent
constraints for the structure constants
C_{\text{NS}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}),\tilde{C}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})
– i.e. without involving the degenerate momentum
p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}
– implied by (
270
). We will proceed analogously to the case of bosonic Liouville in Appendix
B
. In manipulating the constraints (
270
) our strategy is again to eliminate the structure constants that involve the degenerate momentum
p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}
.

In preparation for the above, let us first obtain relations that involve only
c_{1}^{(s)},c_{3}^{(s)}
. We can do that very simply by adding or subtracting appropriate linear combinations of two out of the three equations implied by (
270
). Let us, for example, subtract the following combinations of the two constraints from rows 1 and 2 of
M
:

\displaystyle c^{(s)}_{1}M_{11}+c^{(s)}_{2}M_{12}+c^{(s)}_{3}M_{13}

\displaystyle=0

(272)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{22}}{\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{23}}\times\left(c^{(s)}_{1}M_{21}+c^{(s)}_{2}M_{22}+c^{(s)}_{3}M_{23}\right)

\displaystyle=0

(273)

resulting, after squaring both sides, to

\frac{C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1}+b,p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1}+b)}{C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1}-b,p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1}-b)}=\frac{C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{1}-b)^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1}-b)}{C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{1}+b)^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1}+b)}\times\mathsf{M}^{2}~.

(274)

Here

\mathsf{M}\equiv\left(\frac{\mathcal{L}_{(-)}}{\mathcal{L}_{(+)}}\right)^{2}\frac{\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{31}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{33}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{22}-\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{31}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{23}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{32}}{\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{11}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{12}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{23}-\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{11}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{13}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{22}}

(275)

is a purely kinematic and known quantity. The ratio on the LHS of (
274
) is the first unambiguous bootstrap datum that we are interested in computing eventually. However, there is still an unknown ratio on the RHS that we need to determine and we will discuss that shortly. It is obvious at this stage that we could have obtained relations involving only
c_{1}^{(s)},c_{3}^{(s)}
by combining instead constraints coming from rows 2 and 3, or, 1 and 3 of
M
in a similar manner. However, one quickly realizes that the two latter cases boil down back to the same equation (
274
)
49
49

49

This is due to some non-trivial relations between the matrix elements of
M
, which we leave as homework exercise to the interested reader.
.

The second thing we can do is to obtain relations between
c_{2}^{(s)}
and
c_{3}^{(s)}
. Once we have those, and together with the previous relations between
c_{1}^{(s)},c_{3}^{(s)}
, it is obvious that we can straightforwardly generate the remaining relations between
c_{1}^{(s)},c_{2}^{(s)}
. Working similarly as before, we can subtract the following combinations of the two constraints from rows 1 and 2 of
M
:

\displaystyle c^{(s)}_{1}M_{11}+c^{(s)}_{2}M_{12}+c^{(s)}_{3}M_{13}

\displaystyle=0

(276)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{12}}{\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{13}}\times\left(c^{(s)}_{1}M_{21}+c^{(s)}_{2}M_{22}+c^{(s)}_{3}M_{23}\right)

\displaystyle=0

(277)

resulting, after squaring both sides, to

\frac{\tilde{C}_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1})}{C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1}-b,p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1}-b)}=\frac{C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{1}-b)^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1}-b)}{\tilde{C}_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{1})^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1})}\times\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}^{2}~,

(278)

where now

\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}\equiv\left(\frac{\mathcal{L}_{(-)}}{\mathcal{L}_{(0)}}\right)^{2}\frac{\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{31}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{32}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{13}-\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{31}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{33}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{12}}{\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{21}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{22}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{13}-\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{21}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{23}\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{12}}.

(279)

The ratio on the LHS of (
278
) is the second unambiguous bootstrap datum that we are interested in computing, barring the undetermined ratio involving the degenerate momentum
p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}
on the RHS. It is again easy to verify that the relations between
c^{(s)}_{2},c^{(s)}_{3}
coming from rows 2 and 3, or, 1 and 3 of
M
under the same manipulations end up being the same as (
278
).

Let us now determine the unknown ratios on the RHS of (
274
), (
278
) and write down the final form of the shift relations in the NS-sector. We can do this easily by setting
p_{2}=p_{\langle 1,3\rangle}
and
p_{3}=p_{1}
in (
270
). We first find

\left.\text{det}\ M\right|_{p_{2}\rightarrow p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{3}\rightarrow p_{1}}=0

(280)

which means that this particular matrix is not full rank. One can actually see that it is of rank 2. The vector
\left.\vec{c}\right|_{p_{2}\rightarrow p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{3}\rightarrow p_{1}}
involves the
squares
of the structures constants
C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{1}\pm b),\tilde{C}_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{1})
(as well as the corresponding two-point functions) which are exactly the missing pieces in (
274
), (
278
). By performing the same manipulations as before, namely taking the subtraction of

\displaystyle\left.c^{(s)}_{1}M_{11}+c^{(s)}_{2}M_{12}+c^{(s)}_{3}M_{13}\right|_{p_{2}\rightarrow p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{3}\rightarrow p_{1}}

\displaystyle=0~,

(281)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{22}}{\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{23}}\times\left(c^{(s)}_{1}M_{21}+c^{(s)}_{2}M_{22}+c^{(s)}_{3}M_{23}\right)\right|_{p_{2}\rightarrow p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{3}\rightarrow p_{1}}

\displaystyle=0~,

(282)

as well as the subtraction of

\displaystyle\left.c^{(s)}_{1}M_{11}+c^{(s)}_{2}M_{12}+c^{(s)}_{3}M_{13}\right|_{p_{2}\rightarrow p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{3}\rightarrow p_{1}}

\displaystyle=0~,

(283)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{12}}{\mathbb{F}^{(\text{NS})}_{13}}\times\left(c^{(s)}_{1}M_{21}+c^{(s)}_{2}M_{22}+c^{(s)}_{3}M_{23}\right)\right|_{p_{2}\rightarrow p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{3}\rightarrow p_{1}}

\displaystyle=0~,

(284)

we obtain respectively

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{1}-b)^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1}-b)}{C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{1}+b)^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1}+b)}=\left(\left.\mathsf{M}\right|_{p_{2}\rightarrow p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{3}\rightarrow p_{1}}\right)^{-1}~,

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{1}-b)^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1}-b)}{\tilde{C}_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{1})^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1})}=\left(\left.\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}\right|_{p_{2}\rightarrow p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{3}\rightarrow p_{1}}\right)^{-1}.

(285)

Combining (
274
), (
278
) with (
C
), we eventually get the two desired shift relations

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1}+b,p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1}+b)}{C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1}-b,p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1}-b)}=\frac{\mathsf{M}^{2}}{\left.\mathsf{M}\right|_{p_{2}\rightarrow p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{3}\rightarrow p_{1}}}\equiv\kappa^{(\text{NS})}_{b}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3}),

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{C}_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1})}{C_{\text{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{1}-b,p_{2},p_{3})^{2}/B^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{1}-b)}=\frac{\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}^{2}}{\left.\widetilde{\mathsf{M}}\right|_{p_{2}\rightarrow p_{\langle 1,3\rangle},p_{3}\rightarrow p_{1}}}\equiv\lambda^{(\text{NS})}_{b}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3}),

(286)

where the explicit form of
\kappa^{(\text{NS})},\lambda^{(\text{NS})}
read

\displaystyle\kappa^{(\text{NS})}_{b}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3})=-\frac{\gamma\left(-b^{2}+bp_{1}\right)}{\gamma\left(-b^{2}-bp_{1}\right)}\left[\frac{\Gamma(bp_{1})}{\Gamma(-bp_{1})}\frac{\gamma\left(\frac{1-b^{2}}{2}+bp_{1}\right)}{\gamma\left(\frac{1-b^{2}}{2}-bp_{1}\right)}\frac{\gamma\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(1-b^{2}-2bp_{1-2-3}\right)\right)}{\gamma\left(\frac{1}{4}(1-b^{2}+2bp_{1-2-3})\right)}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~\times\left[\frac{\gamma\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(1-b^{2}-2bp_{1+2-3}\right)\right)\gamma\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(1-b^{2}-2bp_{1-2+3}\right)\right)\gamma\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(1-b^{2}-2bp_{1+2+3}\right)\right)}{\gamma\left(\frac{1}{4}(1-b^{2}+2bp_{1+2-3})\right)\gamma\left(\frac{1}{4}(1-b^{2}+2bp_{1-2+3})\right)\gamma\left(\frac{1}{4}(1-b^{2}+2bp_{1+2+3})\right)}\right]^{2}~,

(287)

\displaystyle\lambda^{(\text{NS})}_{b}(p_{1}|p_{2},p_{3})=-\frac{4\Gamma\left(1+bp_{1}\right)}{b^{4}\Gamma\left(-bp_{1}\right)}\frac{\gamma\left(-b^{2}+bp_{1}\right)}{\gamma\left(\frac{1+b^{2}}{2}-bp_{1}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\left[\frac{\gamma\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(3+b^{2}-2bp_{1-2-3}\right)\right)\gamma\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(3+b^{2}-2bp_{1+2-3}\right)\right)}{\gamma\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(1-b^{2}+2bp_{1-2+3}\right)\right)\gamma\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(1-b^{2}+2bp_{1+2+3}\right)\right)}\right]^{2}~.

(288)

with
\gamma(x)=\Gamma(x)/\Gamma(1-x)
and e.g.
p_{1+2-3}=p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3}
etc. This concludes the derivation of the shift relations in the NS-sector.

Appendix D
Derivation of the shift relations in the R-sector

We start as in the non-supersymmetric and NS-sector cases with the null vector equation. In the R-sector the level 1 null vector is (
14
). In the following we derive, as in the NS-sector, two coupled differential equations which we will use to obtain a single second-order (hypergeometric-type) differential equation for

\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle~.

(289)

The reader not interested in all the detailed calculations leading up to the main differential equation can proceed to the discussion after (
373
).

1st equation.

We start with the following expression
50
50

50

We recall the behaviour of
G(z)
as
z\rightarrow\infty
from footnote
46
.

\displaystyle\oint_{\infty}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\langle G(z)G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle=0~,

(290)

where

\varepsilon(z)\equiv\sqrt{\frac{(z-z_{1})}{(z-z_{0})(z_{0}-z_{1})}}~.

(291)

The square root
\sqrt{\frac{z-z_{1}}{z-z_{0}}}
avoids branch cuts in the OPE of the supercurrent
G(z)
with the Ramond fields, while the exta
\sqrt{z_{0}-z_{1}}
is merely a cosmetical contribution.
Deforming the contour and using

\displaystyle G(\omega)V_{p}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\epsilon V_{p}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon}G(\omega)~,\quad G(\omega)G_{0}V_{p}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon}=-\epsilon G_{0}V_{p}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon}G(\omega)~,

(292)

\displaystyle G(\omega)G_{0}^{2}V_{p}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\epsilon G_{0}^{2}V_{p}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon}G(\omega)

(293)

we obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\oint_{z_{0}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\langle\big[G(z)G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})\big]_{\mathrm{OPE}}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle

(294)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon_{0}\oint_{z_{1}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\langle G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})\big[G(z)V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\big]_{\mathrm{OPE}}V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle

(295)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\oint_{z_{2}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\langle G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\big[G(z)V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{2}}(z_{2})\big]_{\mathrm{OPE}}V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\rangle

(296)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\oint_{z_{3}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\langle G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{2}}(z_{2})\big[G(z)V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\big]_{\mathrm{OPE}}\rangle~.

(297)

Using the OPE between the supercurrent and the NS- and R-fields respectively, in particular

G(z)V_{p}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon}\sim\frac{1}{z^{3/2}}G_{0}V_{p}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon}+\frac{1}{z^{1/2}}G_{-1}V_{p}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon}~,

(298)

the above leads to

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\oint_{z_{0}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\bigg\langle\frac{G_{0}^{2}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}}{(z-z_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}}}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\bigg\rangle

(299)

\displaystyle\quad+\oint_{z_{0}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\bigg\langle\frac{G_{-1}G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})}{(z-z_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\bigg\rangle

(300)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon\oint_{z_{1}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\bigg\langle G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})\frac{G_{0}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})}{(z-z_{1})^{\frac{3}{2}}}V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{2}}(z_{2})V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\bigg\rangle

(301)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon\epsilon_{1}\oint_{z_{2}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\left\langle G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\frac{\Lambda_{p_{2}}(z_{2})}{(z-z_{2})}V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{3}}(z_{3})\right\rangle

(302)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon\epsilon_{1}\oint_{z_{3}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\left\langle G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})\frac{\Lambda^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{3}}(z_{3})}{(z-z_{3})}\right\rangle~.

(303)

Now using
\varepsilon(z)
given in (
291
) we obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2z_{01}}\langle G_{0}^{2}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle-\langle G_{-1}G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

(304)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\epsilon_{1}\sqrt{\frac{z_{21}}{z_{01}z_{20}}}\langle G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

(305)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\epsilon_{1}\sqrt{\frac{z_{31}}{z_{01}z_{30}}}\langle G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})\Lambda_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle~

(306)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{01}}\langle G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})G_{0}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle~.

(307)

Note that we have

G_{0}V_{p}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon}=i\beta\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i\epsilon}{4}}V_{p}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon}~\Rightarrow G_{0}^{2}R_{a}^{\epsilon}=-\beta^{2}V_{p}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon}~,\quad\beta\equiv\frac{p}{\sqrt{2}}~.

(308)

Using the R-sector null vector (
14
) we have

\displaystyle\kappa\partial_{z_{0}}\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle=\langle G_{-1}G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle~,

(309)

with
\kappa\equiv\frac{2b^{2}+1}{2b^{2}}
. We then replace the right hand side with (
304
) which leads to

\displaystyle\kappa\partial_{z_{0}}\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle=-\frac{1}{2z_{01}}\langle G_{0}^{2}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

(310)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\epsilon_{1}\sqrt{\frac{z_{21}}{z_{01}z_{20}}}\langle G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

(311)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\epsilon_{1}\sqrt{\frac{z_{31}}{z_{01}z_{30}}}\langle G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})\Lambda_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

(312)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{01}}\langle G_{0}V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})G_{0}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle~.

(313)

Next we look at

\oint_{\infty}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\langle G(z)G_{0}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle=0~,\quad\varepsilon(z)\equiv\sqrt{\frac{(z-z_{0})(z-z_{1})}{(z_{3}-z_{0})(z_{3}-z_{1})}}~.

(314)

This choice leads to

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\oint_{z_{0}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\left\langle\frac{G_{0}^{2}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})}{(z-z_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}}}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\right\rangle

(315)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon_{0}\oint_{z_{1}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\left\langle V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})\frac{G_{0}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})}{(z-z_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}}}V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\right\rangle

(316)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\oint_{z_{2}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\left\langle G_{0}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\frac{\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})}{(z-z_{2})}V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\right\rangle

(317)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\oint_{z_{3}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\left\langle G_{0}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})\frac{\Lambda_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})}{(z-z_{3})}\right\rangle~,

(318)

or, after evaluating the residues,

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{z_{01}}{z_{30}z_{31}}}\langle G_{0}^{2}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

(319)

\displaystyle-\epsilon_{0}\sqrt{-1}\sqrt{\frac{z_{01}}{z_{30}z_{31}}}\langle G_{0}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})G_{0}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

(320)

\displaystyle-\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\sqrt{\frac{z_{20}z_{21}}{z_{30}z_{31}}}\langle G_{0}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

(321)

\displaystyle-\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\langle G_{0}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})\Lambda_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle~.

(322)

We therefore obtain

\displaystyle\kappa\partial_{z_{0}}g^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})=\frac{\beta_{p_{0}}^{2}}{2z_{01}}g^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})+\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\sqrt{\frac{z_{21}}{z_{01}z_{20}}}h^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})-\epsilon_{0}\beta_{p_{0}}\beta_{p_{1}}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{01}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{4}(\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1})}g^{-\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})

(323)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon\epsilon_{1}\sqrt{\frac{z_{20}z_{21}}{z_{01}z_{30}^{2}}}h^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})+\epsilon_{1}\beta_{p_{0}}\beta_{p_{1}}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{30}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{4}(\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1})}g^{-\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})-\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\frac{\beta_{p_{0}}^{2}}{z_{30}}g^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})~,

(324)

where
p_{0}=p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}
and

\displaystyle g^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})

(325)

\displaystyle h^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle\equiv\langle G_{0}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle~.

We will denote (

) as the first equation in the R-sector.

2nd equation.

For the second equation we start with

\displaystyle=-\oint_{z_{0}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\langle\big[G(z)G_{0}^{2}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})\big]_{\mathrm{OPE}}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle

(328)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon_{0}\oint_{z_{1}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\langle G_{0}^{2}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})\big[G(z)V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\big]_{\mathrm{OPE}}\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\oint_{z_{2}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\langle G_{0}^{2}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\big[G(z)\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})\big]_{\mathrm{OPE}}V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

(330)

Using the OPE between the supercurrent and the NS- and R-fields respectively we obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=-\oint_{z_{0}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\bigg\langle\frac{G_{0}G_{0}^{2}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})}{(z-z_{0})^{\frac{3}{2}}}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\bigg\rangle

(332)

\displaystyle\quad-\oint_{z_{0}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\bigg\langle\frac{G_{-1}G_{0}^{2}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})}{(z-z_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\bigg\rangle

(333)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon_{0}\oint_{z_{1}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\bigg\langle G_{0}^{2}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})\frac{G_{0}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})}{(z-z_{1})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\oint_{z_{2}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\left\langle G_{0}^{2}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\left(\frac{2h_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}}{(z-z_{2})^{2}}+\frac{1}{(z-z_{2})}\partial_{z_{2}}\right)V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\right\rangle

(335)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\oint_{z_{3}}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\,\left\langle G_{0}^{2}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})\frac{\Lambda_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})}{(z-z_{3})}\right\rangle~.

\varepsilon(z)
from (

(337)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon_{0}\beta_{0}^{2}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{01}}\langle V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})G_{0}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\beta_{0}^{2}h_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\sqrt{\frac{z_{01}}{z_{20}^{3}z_{21}}}\langle V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

To eliminate the last term with two
\Lambda

\oint_{\infty}\frac{\text{d}z}{2\pi i}\varepsilon(z)\langle G(z)G_{0}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle=0~,\quad\varepsilon(z)\equiv\sqrt{\frac{(z-z_{0})(z-z_{1})}{(z_{3}-z_{0})(z_{3}-z_{1})}}~,

which leads to

\displaystyle\langle V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})\Lambda_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle=-h_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\frac{z_{02}+z_{12}}{\sqrt{z_{30}z_{20}z_{31}z_{21}}}\langle V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

(344)

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\frac{z_{20}z_{21}}{z_{30}z_{31}}}\partial_{z_{2}}\langle V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{2}(z_{2})V_{3}(z_{3})\rangle

(345)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\sqrt{\frac{z_{01}}{z_{30}z_{31}}}\langle G_{0}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

(346)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon_{1}\sqrt{-1}\sqrt{\frac{z_{01}}{z_{31}z_{30}}}\langle V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})G_{0}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle~.

Combining the above equation with the R-sector null-vector (

(348)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon_{0}\beta\beta_{1}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{01}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{4}(\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1})}h^{-\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})-\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\beta_{0}^{2}h_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\sqrt{\frac{z_{01}}{z_{20}^{3}z_{21}}}g^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})

(349)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\beta_{0}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{z_{21}}{z_{20}z_{01}}}\partial_{z_{2}}g^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})-\epsilon_{0}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{30}}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{4}(\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1})}h^{-\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})

(350)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{z_{30}}h^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})-\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\beta_{0}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{z_{20}z_{21}}{z_{01}z_{30}^{2}}}\partial_{z_{2}}g^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})

(351)

\displaystyle\quad-\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{1}\beta_{0}^{2}h_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\frac{z_{20}+z_{21}}{\sqrt{{z_{01}z_{30}^{2}z_{20}z_{21}}}}g^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})~.

(352)

Note that here we used

\displaystyle\beta_{0}\langle V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})G_{0}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

(353)

\displaystyle=-i\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{4}\epsilon_{0}}\langle G_{0}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},-\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})G_{0}V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

(354)

\displaystyle=-i\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{4}\epsilon_{0}}(i\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{4}\epsilon_{1}})\beta_{1}\langle G_{0}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},-\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},-\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle

(355)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{4}(\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1})}\beta_{1}h^{-\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})~.

(356)

This gives us the second equation (
348
).

We hence encounter the following different cases:

\epsilon_{0}=\epsilon_{1}
.

\displaystyle\kappa\partial_{z_{0}}g^{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{2z_{01}}g^{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})+\sqrt{\frac{z_{21}}{z_{01}z_{20}}}h^{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})-\epsilon_{0}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{01}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{2}\epsilon_{0}}g^{-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})

(357a)

\displaystyle\quad-\sqrt{\frac{z_{20}z_{21}}{z_{01}z_{30}^{2}}}h^{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})+\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\epsilon_{0}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{30}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{2}\epsilon_{0}}g^{-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})-\frac{\beta^{2}}{z_{30}}g^{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})~,

(357b)

\displaystyle\kappa\partial_{z_{0}}h^{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{2z_{01}}h^{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})+\epsilon_{0}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{01}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{2}\epsilon_{0}}h^{-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})-\beta_{0}^{2}h_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\sqrt{\frac{z_{01}}{z_{20}^{3}z_{21}}}g^{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})

(357c)

\displaystyle\quad+\beta_{0}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{z_{21}}{z_{20}z_{01}}}\partial_{z_{2}}g^{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})-\epsilon_{0}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{30}}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{2}\epsilon_{0}}h^{-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})-\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{z_{30}}h^{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})

(357d)

\displaystyle\quad-\beta_{0}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{z_{20}z_{21}}{z_{01}z_{30}^{2}}}\partial_{z_{2}}g^{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})-\beta_{0}^{2}h_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\frac{z_{20}+z_{21}}{\sqrt{{z_{01}z_{30}^{2}z_{20}z_{21}}}}g^{\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})~.
