\epsilon_{0}=-\epsilon_{1}
.

In this case,

\displaystyle\kappa\partial_{z_{0}}g^{\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta_{p_{0}}^{2}}{2z_{01}}g^{\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})-\sqrt{\frac{z_{21}}{z_{01}z_{20}}}h^{\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})-\epsilon_{0}\beta_{p_{0}}\beta_{p_{1}}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{01}}g^{-\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})

(358a)

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\frac{z_{20}z_{21}}{z_{01}z_{30}^{2}}}h^{\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})-\epsilon_{0}\beta_{p_{0}}\beta_{p_{1}}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{30}}g^{-\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})+\frac{\beta_{p_{0}}^{2}}{z_{30}}g^{\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})~,

(358b)

\displaystyle\kappa\partial_{z_{0}}h^{\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{2z_{01}}h^{\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})+\epsilon_{0}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{01}}h^{-\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})+\beta_{0}^{2}h_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\sqrt{\frac{z_{01}}{z_{20}^{3}z_{21}}}g^{\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})

(358c)

\displaystyle\quad-\beta_{0}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{z_{21}}{z_{20}z_{01}}}\partial_{z_{2}}g^{\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})-\epsilon_{0}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z_{30}}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}h^{-\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})-\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{z_{30}}h^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})

(358d)

\displaystyle\quad+\beta_{0}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{z_{20}z_{21}}{z_{01}z_{30}^{2}}}\partial_{z_{2}}g^{\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})+\beta_{0}^{2}h_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\frac{z_{20}+z_{21}}{\sqrt{{z_{01}z_{30}^{2}z_{20}z_{21}}}}g^{\epsilon_{0},-\epsilon_{0}}(\mathbf{z})~.

(358e)

As a reminder,

\displaystyle g^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle\equiv\langle V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle~,

(359)

\displaystyle h^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle\equiv\langle G_{0}V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(z_{1})\Lambda_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{2})V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(z_{3})\rangle~.

(360)

We now make the ansatz

g^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})=\prod_{i<j}z_{ij}^{\mu_{ij}}g_{0}^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(z)~,\quad h^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})=\prod_{i<j}z_{ij}^{\nu_{ij}}h_{0}^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(z)~,

(361)

where
z\equiv\frac{z_{01}z_{23}}{z_{03}z_{21}}
is the cross-ratio. In the limit

z_{0}\rightarrow z~,\quad z_{1}\rightarrow 0~,\quad z_{2}\rightarrow 1~,\quad z_{3}\rightarrow\infty

(362)

the derivatives transform as

\partial_{z_{0}}=\frac{\mu_{01}}{z}+\frac{\mu_{02}}{z-1}+\partial_{z}~,\quad\partial_{z_{2}}=-\frac{\mu_{02}}{z-1}+\mu_{12}-z\partial_{z}

(363)

when acting on
g^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})
, and with
\mu\leftrightarrow\nu
when we act on
h^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(\mathbf{z})
. Finally performing the by now standard replacement

g_{0}^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(z)\rightarrow z^{-\mu_{01}}(1-z)^{-\mu_{02}}g_{0}^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(z)~,\quad h_{0}^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(z)\rightarrow z^{-\nu_{01}}(1-z)^{-\nu_{02}}h_{0}^{\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}}(z)

(364)

we find for
\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{0}
:

\displaystyle\kappa\partial_{z}g_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{2z}g_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}(z)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{z(1-z)}}\,h_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}(z)-\epsilon_{0}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{2}\epsilon_{0}}g_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}(z)~,

(365a)

\displaystyle\kappa\partial_{z}h_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{2z}h_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}(z)+\epsilon_{0}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{z}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{2}\epsilon_{0}}h_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}(z)-\beta_{0}^{2}h_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\sqrt{\frac{z}{(1-z)^{3}}}\,g_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}(z)

(365b)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{\sqrt{z(1-z)}}(\mu_{01}+\mu_{02}+\mu_{12}-z\partial_{z})g_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}(z)~.

(365c)

In principle we also have an equation for
\epsilon_{1}=-\epsilon_{0}
, however it can be shown that none of the solutions of that differential equation satisfy crossing
Fukuda:2002bv
and hence we will not discuss this situation further.

Focusing on the case
\epsilon_{0}=\epsilon_{1}
we now fix the branch of the square root. For this we look at the first equation in (
365
) in the limit where
V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}
and
V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}
are the identity. We also use that
\epsilon_{0}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi i}{2}\epsilon_{0}}=-i
. This then leads to two options:

\kappa\partial_{z}\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z)V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(0)\rangle=\frac{\beta^{2}}{2z}\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z)V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(0)\rangle\pm\frac{\beta^{2}}{z}\langle V_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\mathrm{R},-\epsilon_{0}}(z)V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},-\epsilon_{1}}(0)\rangle~,

(366)

Using that the two point function is of the form

\langle V_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{0}}(z)V_{p_{1}}^{\mathrm{R},\epsilon_{1}}(0)\rangle=\frac{C}{z^{2h_{[p_{0}]}}}\delta(p_{0}-p_{1})~,

(367)

where
C
is independent of the coordinates we see that only the
+
sign makes sense. Therefore, in summary, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{I})\quad\kappa\partial_{z}g_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{2z}g_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}(z)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{z(1-z)}}\,h_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}(z)+\frac{\beta_{0}\beta_{1}}{z}g_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}(z)~,

(368a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{II})\quad\kappa\partial_{z}h_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{2z}h_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}(z)-\frac{\beta_{0}\beta_{1}}{z}h_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}(z)-\beta_{0}^{2}h_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\sqrt{\frac{z}{(1-z)^{3}}}\,g_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}(z)

(368b)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{\sqrt{z(1-z)}}(\mu_{01}+\mu_{02}+\mu_{12}-z\partial_{z})g_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}(z)~,

(368c)

where

\mu_{01}+\mu_{02}+\mu_{12}=-h^{\mathrm{R}}_{[p_{0}]}-h^{\mathrm{R}}_{[p_{1}]}-h^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{2}}+h^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{3}}~.

(369)

The sign in the second equation is fixed by taking
V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}
to the identity and using the general form of the OPE

\displaystyle\left[V^{\text{R},\epsilon}\right]\left[V^{\text{R},\epsilon}\right]\sim\left[V\right]+\left[W\right]\ ,\ \ \left[V^{\text{R},\epsilon}\right]\left[V^{\text{R},-\epsilon}\right]\sim\left[\Lambda\right]+\left[\widetilde{\Lambda}\right]\ ,~~~~~~\epsilon=\pm~.

(370)

Adding
\mathrm{I})
and
\mathrm{II})
to themselves with
\epsilon_{0}\rightarrow-\epsilon_{0}
, and with

\mathsf{g}^{\epsilon_{0}}=\frac{1}{2}(\epsilon_{0}g_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}+g_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}})~,\quad\mathsf{h}^{\epsilon_{0}}=\frac{1}{2}(\epsilon_{0}h_{0}^{\epsilon_{0}}+h_{0}^{-\epsilon_{0}}),

(371)

we obtain

\displaystyle\kappa\partial_{z}\mathsf{g}^{\epsilon}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{2z}\mathsf{g}^{\epsilon}(z)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{z(1-z)}}\mathsf{h}^{\epsilon}(z)+\epsilon\frac{\beta_{0}\beta_{1}}{z}\mathsf{g}^{\epsilon}(z)~,

(372a)

\displaystyle\kappa\partial_{z}\mathsf{h}^{\epsilon}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{2z}\mathsf{h}^{\epsilon}(z)-\epsilon\frac{\beta_{0}\beta_{1}}{z}\mathsf{h}^{\epsilon}(z)-\beta_{0}^{2}h_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\sqrt{\frac{z}{(1-z)^{3}}}\,\mathsf{g}^{\epsilon}(z)

(372b)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{\sqrt{z(1-z)}}(\mu_{01}+\mu_{02}+\mu_{12}-z\partial_{z})\mathsf{g}^{\epsilon}(z)~,

(372c)

where we replaced
\epsilon_{0}=\epsilon
and
\beta\equiv\frac{p}{\sqrt{2}}
with
p_{0}=p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}
and
\kappa\equiv\frac{2b^{2}+1}{2b^{2}}
. This is the final form of the equations that we will now study in order to obtain the R-sector shift relations.

Shift-equation.

We can eliminate
\mathsf{h}^{\epsilon}
in (
372
) by solving for it in the first equation and plugging it into the second. Making the ansatz

\mathsf{g}^{\epsilon}(z)=z^{\frac{1}{8}+\frac{b^{2}}{4}+\epsilon\frac{bp_{1}}{2}}(1-z)^{\frac{b}{2}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{2}\right)}F(z)~,

(373)

we finally obtain the main differential equation

z(1-z)F^{\prime\prime}(z)+(c_{\epsilon}-(a_{\epsilon}+b_{\epsilon}+1)z)F^{\prime}(z)-a_{\epsilon}b_{\epsilon}F(z)=0~,

(374)

where

a_{\epsilon}=\frac{1}{4}-\frac{b}{2}(-\epsilon p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})~,\quad b_{\epsilon}=\frac{1}{4}-\frac{b}{2}(-\epsilon p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3})~,\quad c_{\epsilon}=\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon bp_{1}~.

(375)

We have two linearly independent solutions for each
\epsilon
. In the s-channel these are given by

\mathsf{g}_{s}^{\epsilon}(z)=z^{\frac{1}{8}+\frac{b^{2}}{4}+\epsilon\frac{bp_{1}}{2}}(1-z)^{\frac{b}{2}(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{2})}\,_{2}F_{1}\left(a_{\epsilon},b_{\epsilon},c_{\epsilon};z\right)\cr+z^{\frac{5}{8}+\frac{b^{2}}{4}-\epsilon\frac{bp_{1}}{2}}(1-z)^{\frac{b}{2}(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{2})}\,_{2}F_{1}\left(a_{\epsilon}-c_{\epsilon}+1,b_{\epsilon}-c_{\epsilon}+1,2-c_{\epsilon};z\right)~.

(376)

Similarly we have two linearly independent solutions (for each
\epsilon
) in the
t
-channel

\mathsf{g}_{t}^{\epsilon}(z)=z^{\frac{1}{8}+\frac{b^{2}}{4}+\epsilon\frac{bp_{1}}{2}}(1-z)^{\frac{b}{2}(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{2})}\,_{2}F_{1}\left(a_{\epsilon},b_{\epsilon},a_{\epsilon}+b_{\epsilon}+1-c_{\epsilon};1-z\right)\cr+z^{\frac{1}{8}+\frac{b^{2}}{4}+\epsilon\frac{bp_{1}}{2}}(1-z)^{\frac{b}{2}(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{2})}\,_{2}F_{1}\left(c_{\epsilon}-a_{\epsilon},c_{\epsilon}-b_{\epsilon},c_{\epsilon}-a_{\epsilon}-b_{\epsilon}+1;1-z\right)~.

(377)

We can read the internal momenta in the
s
-channel from the
z\rightarrow 0
limit. We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}+\frac{b^{2}}{4}+\epsilon\frac{bp_{1}}{2}

\displaystyle=-\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{8}-\frac{p^{2}_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}{2}+\frac{1}{16}\right)-\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{8}-\frac{p^{2}_{1}}{2}+\frac{1}{16}\right)+\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{8}-\frac{\left(p_{1}-\frac{\epsilon b}{2}\right)^{2}}{2}\right)~

(378a)

\displaystyle\frac{5}{8}+\frac{b^{2}}{4}-\epsilon\frac{bp_{1}}{2}

\displaystyle=-\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{8}-\frac{p^{2}_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}{2}+\frac{1}{16}\right)-\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{8}-\frac{p^{2}_{1}}{2}+\frac{1}{16}\right)+\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{8}-\frac{\left(p_{1}+\frac{\epsilon b}{2}\right)^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)~.

(378b)

Similarly, in the
t
-channel we can read off the internal momenta in the
z\rightarrow 1
limit

\frac{b}{2}\left(\frac{Q}{2}\mp p_{2}\right)=-\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{8}-\frac{p_{\langle 2,1\rangle}^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{16}\right)-\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{8}-\frac{p_{2}^{2}}{2}\right)+\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{8}-\frac{(p_{2}\pm\frac{b}{2})^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{16}\right)~.

(379)

Since the exponent of
(1-z)
in
\mathsf{g}_{t}^{\epsilon}(z)
is independent of
\epsilon
, (
379
) is valid for either
\epsilon=\pm 1
.

Note that, since
V_{p}^{\mathrm{R},+}
is a combination of the two operators
\Theta^{\pm\pm}
,
\mathsf{g}^{+}(z)
encodes the four-point functions

\langle\Theta^{\pm\pm}_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}(z)\Theta^{\pm\pm}_{p_{1}}(0)V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(1)V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(\infty)\rangle~,\quad\langle\Theta^{\pm\pm}_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}(z)\Theta^{\mp\mp}_{p_{1}}(0)V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(1)V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(\infty)\rangle~.

(380)

Similarly, since
V_{p}^{\mathrm{R},-}
is a combination of the two operators
\Theta^{\pm\mp}
,
\mathsf{g}^{-}(z)
encodes the four-point functions

\langle\Theta^{\pm\mp}_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}(z)\Theta^{\pm\mp}_{p_{1}}(0)V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(1)V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(\infty)\rangle~,\quad\langle\Theta^{\pm\mp}_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}(z)\Theta^{\mp\pm}_{p_{1}}(0)V_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(1)V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}(\infty)\rangle~.

(381)

We now discuss the case
\epsilon=+1
(i.e. (
380
)). The case
\epsilon=-1
follows analogously. We denote as

C_{\mathrm{odd}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{2};p_{3})\equiv\langle\Theta^{\pm\pm}_{p_{1}}\Theta^{\pm\pm}_{p_{2}}V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\rangle~,\quad C_{\mathrm{even}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{2};p_{3})\equiv\langle\Theta^{\pm\pm}_{p_{1}}\Theta^{\mp\mp}_{p_{2}}V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\rangle~,

(382)

and from the OPEs
Fukuda:2002bv

\displaystyle\left[\Theta_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\pm\pm}\right]\left[\Theta_{p}^{\pm\pm}\right]

\displaystyle\sim\left[\Theta_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\pm\mp}\right]\left[\Theta_{p}^{\pm\mp}\right]=[V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p+\frac{b}{2}}]+[W^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p-\frac{b}{2}}]~,

(383)

\displaystyle\left[\Theta_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\pm\pm}\right]\left[\Theta_{p}^{\mp\mp}\right]

\displaystyle\sim\left[\Theta_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{\pm\mp}\right]\left[\Theta_{p}^{\mp\pm}\right]=[V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p-\frac{b}{2}}]+[W^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p+\frac{b}{2}}]~,

(384)

\displaystyle[\Theta^{\pm\pm}_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}V_{p}^{\mathrm{NS}}]

\displaystyle\sim[\Theta_{p+\frac{b}{2}}^{\pm\pm}]+[\Theta_{p-\frac{b}{2}}^{\mp\mp}]~,

(385)

it is clear that
C_{\mathrm{odd}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{2};p_{3})\propto\langle\Theta^{\pm\mp}_{p_{1}}\Theta^{\pm\mp}_{p_{2}}V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\rangle
and
C_{\mathrm{even}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{2};p_{3})\propto\langle\Theta^{\pm\mp}_{p_{1}}\Theta^{\mp\pm}_{p_{2}}V_{p_{3}}^{\mathrm{NS}}\rangle
. Consequently, we can expand

\displaystyle\langle\Theta_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{++}\Theta_{p_{1}}^{--}V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{2}}V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{3}}\rangle

\displaystyle=c_{1}^{(s)}f_{1,s}(z)\overline{f_{1,s}(z)}+c_{2}^{(s)}f_{2,s}(z)\overline{f_{2,s}(z)}~

(386)

\displaystyle=c_{1}^{(t)}f_{1,t}(z)\overline{f_{1,t}(z)}+c_{2}^{(t)}f_{2,t}(z)\overline{f_{2,t}(z)}~,

(387)

where

\displaystyle c_{1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{(b)}_{\mathrm{odd}}\left(p_{\langle 1,2\rangle},p_{2}+\frac{b}{2};p_{2}\right)C^{(b)}_{\mathrm{even}}\left(p_{2}+\frac{b}{2},p_{1};p_{3}\right)}{B_{\mathrm{R}}^{(b)}(p_{2}+\frac{b}{2})}

(388)

\displaystyle c_{2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{(b)}_{\mathrm{even}}\left(p_{\langle 1,2\rangle},p_{2}-\frac{b}{2};p_{2}\right)C^{(b)}_{\mathrm{odd}}\left(p_{2}-\frac{b}{2},p_{1};p_{3}\right)}{B_{\mathrm{R}}^{(b)}(p_{2}-\frac{b}{2})}~.

(389)

We note in passing that from the OPE (
385
) one can infer the property

C_{\mathrm{even}}^{(b)}(p_{1},-p_{2};p_{3})=C_{\mathrm{odd}}^{(b)}(p_{1},p_{2};p_{3})=C_{\mathrm{even}}^{(b)}(-p_{1},p_{2};p_{3})~.

(390)

\Theta^{--}_{p_{1}}
V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{3}}
V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{2}}
\Theta^{++}_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}
h_{[p_{2}+\frac{\eta b}{2}]}
\Theta^{\eta\eta}_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}
\Theta_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{++}
\Theta_{p_{1}}^{--}
V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{3}}
V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{2}}
~~~~~~~~=\ \sum_{\eta=\pm}{\mathbb{F}}_{\delta,\eta}
h_{p_{1}-\delta\frac{p_{1}}{2}}

Figure 10
:
Crossing transformation of sphere four-point conformal blocks with one insertion of a
\langle 1,2\rangle
degenerate R-sector operator. The fusion kernel is a
2\times 2
matrix given in (
393
).

We now impose crossing in (
386
), which implies
c_{1}^{(t)}{\mathbb{F}}_{11}{\mathbb{F}}_{12}+c_{2}^{(t)}{\mathbb{F}}_{21}{\mathbb{F}}_{22}=0
. The matrix entries
{\mathbb{F}}_{ij}
follow again from the basic properties of the hypergeometric function, i.e.

{}_{2}F_{1}\left(a,f,c,1-z\right)=\frac{\Gamma(c)\Gamma(c-a-f)}{\Gamma(c-a)\Gamma(c-f)}\,_{2}F_{1}(a,f,a+f-c+1,z)\cr+\frac{\Gamma(c)\Gamma(a+f-c)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(f)}z^{c-a-f}\,_{2}F_{1}(c-a,c-f,c-a-f+1,z)~.

(391)

These are explicitly given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-bp_{1})\Gamma(1-bp_{2})}{\Gamma(\frac{3}{4}-\frac{b}{2}p_{1+2-3})\Gamma(\frac{3}{4}-\frac{b}{2}p_{1+2+3})}~,\quad\mathbb{F}_{12}=\frac{\Gamma(-\frac{1}{2}+bp_{1})\Gamma(1-bp_{2})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{b}{2}p_{1-2+3})\Gamma(\frac{1}{4}-\frac{b}{2}p_{-1+2+3})}~,

(392)

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}_{21}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(1+bp_{2})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-bp_{1})}{\Gamma(\frac{3}{4}-\frac{b}{2}p_{1-2+3})\Gamma(\frac{3}{4}+\frac{b}{2}p_{-1+2+3})}~,\quad\mathbb{F}_{22}=\frac{\Gamma(1+bp_{2})\Gamma(-\frac{1}{2}+bp_{1})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{b}{2}p_{1+2-3})\Gamma(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{b}{2}p_{1+2+3})}~.

(393)

We thus find

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\mathrm{even}}^{(b)}(p_{2}+\frac{b}{2},p_{1};p_{3})^{2}/B_{\mathrm{R}}^{(b)}(p_{2}+\frac{b}{2})}{C_{\mathrm{odd}}^{(b)}(p_{2}-\frac{b}{2},p_{1};p_{3})^{2}/B_{\mathrm{R}}^{(b)}(p_{2}-\frac{b}{2})}

\displaystyle=-\left(\frac{{\mathbb{F}}_{21}{\mathbb{F}}_{22}}{{\mathbb{F}}_{11}{\mathbb{F}}_{12}}\right)^{\!\!2}\times\left(\frac{{\mathbb{F}}_{11}{\mathbb{F}}_{12}}{{\mathbb{F}}_{22}{\mathbb{F}}_{21}}\bigg|_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}=p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}\\
p_{3}=p_{2}\end{subarray}}\right)~

(394)

\displaystyle\equiv\kappa_{\mathrm{R}}^{(b)}(p_{2}|p_{1};p_{3})~.

(395)

Similarly, crossing symmetry of the following four-point function

\displaystyle\langle\Theta_{p_{\langle 1,2\rangle}}^{++}\Theta_{-p_{1}}^{++}V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{2}}V^{\mathrm{NS}}_{p_{3}}\rangle

\displaystyle=c_{1}^{(s)}f_{1,s}(z)\overline{f_{1,s}(z)}+c_{2}^{(s)}f_{2,s}(z)\overline{f_{2,s}(z)}~

(396)

\displaystyle=c_{1}^{(t)}f_{1,t}(z)\overline{f_{1,t}(z)}+c_{2}^{(t)}f_{2,t}(z)\overline{f_{2,t}(z)}~,

(397)

where now

\displaystyle c_{1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{(b)}_{\mathrm{odd}}\left(p_{\langle 1,2\rangle},p_{2}+\frac{b}{2};p_{2}\right)C^{(b)}_{\mathrm{odd}}\left(p_{2}+\frac{b}{2},p_{1};p_{3}\right)}{B_{\mathrm{R}}^{(b)}(p_{2}+\frac{b}{2})}

(398)

\displaystyle c_{2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{(b)}_{\mathrm{even}}\left(p_{\langle 1,2\rangle},p_{2}-\frac{b}{2};p_{2}\right)C^{(b)}_{\mathrm{even}}\left(p_{2}-\frac{b}{2},p_{1};p_{3}\right)}{B_{\mathrm{R}}^{(b)}(p_{2}-\frac{b}{2})}~,

(399)

yields

\frac{C_{\mathrm{odd}}^{(b)}(p_{2}+\frac{b}{2},p_{1};p_{3})^{2}/B_{\mathrm{R}}^{(b)}(p_{2}+\frac{b}{2})}{C_{\mathrm{even}}^{(b)}(p_{2}-\frac{b}{2},p_{1};p_{3})^{2}/B_{\mathrm{R}}^{(b)}(p_{2}-\frac{b}{2})}=\kappa^{(b)}_{\mathrm{R}}\left(p_{2}|-p_{1};p_{3}\right)~.

(400)

In summary (and after relabelling
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}
appropriately) we find

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\mathrm{even}}^{(b)}(p_{1}+\frac{b}{2},p_{2};p_{3})^{2}/B_{\mathrm{R}}^{(b)}(p_{1}+\frac{b}{2})}{C_{\mathrm{odd}}^{(b)}(p_{1}-\frac{b}{2},p_{2};p_{3})^{2}/B_{\mathrm{R}}^{(b)}(p_{1}-\frac{b}{2})}

\displaystyle=\kappa^{(b)}_{\mathrm{R}}\left(p_{1}|p_{2};p_{3}\right)~,

(401)

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\mathrm{odd}}^{(b)}(p_{1}+\frac{b}{2},p_{2};p_{3})^{2}/B_{\mathrm{R}}^{(b)}(p_{1}+\frac{b}{2})}{C_{\mathrm{even}}^{(b)}(p_{1}-\frac{b}{2},p_{2};p_{3})^{2}/B_{\mathrm{R}}^{(b)}(p_{1}-\frac{b}{2})}

\displaystyle=\kappa^{(b)}_{\mathrm{R}}\left(p_{1}|-p_{2};p_{3}\right)~,

(402)

where
\kappa_{\mathrm{R}}^{(b)}(p_{1}|p_{2};p_{3})
is given in (
72
) in the main text. These are exactly the two shift relations for the normalization-independent bootstrap data on the sphere in the R-sector.

Appendix E
Relation to the
\mathcal{N}=1
fusion kernel

In this last appendix we discuss the fusion kernel of (spacelike)
\mathcal{N}=1
Liouville theory in the NS-sector as obtained in
Hadasz:2007wi
;
Chorazkiewicz:2008es
;
Hadasz:2013bwa
;
Pawelkiewicz:2013wga
;
Poghosyan:2016kvd
. Our motivation is to highlight the fact that the NS Liouville structure constants that we discussed in the main text are realized as particular instances of this fusion kernel, which is in a sense a more general (representation-theoretic) object. This parallels the story in bosonic Liouville where the DOZZ structure constants (in the appropriate normalization) can also be written in terms of the analogous fusion kernel for the usual Virasoro conformal blocks
Teschner:2001rv
;
Collier:2019weq
.

The crossing transformation of an even (e) or odd (o) NS four-point conformal block with four external momenta
p_{1,2,3,4}
51
51

51

See e.g.
Belavin:2007gz
;
Belavin:2007eq
for more details on the construction of the NS conformal blocks. Here the even conformal blocks are normalized as
\mathcal{F}_{p_{s}}^{\mathrm{e}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{3}&p_{2}\\
p_{4}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right](z)=z^{h_{s}-h_{1}-h_{2}}\left(1+O(z)\right)
, while the odd conformal blocks as
\mathcal{F}_{p_{s}}^{\mathrm{o}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{3}&p_{2}\\
p_{4}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right](z)=z^{h_{s}+1/2-h_{1}-h_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2h_{s}}+O(z)\right)
.
defines the
\mathcal{N}=1
NS fusion kernel via

\mathcal{F}_{p_{s}}^{\eta}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{3}&p_{2}\\
p_{4}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right](z)=\int_{i\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{\text{d}p_{t}}{i}\sum_{\rho=e,o}\mathbb{F}_{p_{s}p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{3}&p_{2}\\
p_{4}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\eta}\text{}_{\rho}\ \mathcal{F}_{p_{t}}^{\rho}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{1}&p_{2}\\
p_{4}&p_{3}\end{smallmatrix}\right](1-z)~,\quad~\eta=\mathrm{e},\mathrm{o}.

(403)

The components of the fusion kernel were first written down in
Hadasz:2007wi
;
Chorazkiewicz:2008es
,
and in our notation read explicitly

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}_{p_{s}p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{3}&p_{2}\\
p_{4}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{e}}\text{}_{\mathrm{e}}=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{t}+p_{2}-p_{3}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{t}+p_{2}+p_{3}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{t}-p_{1}-p_{4}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{t}+p_{1}-p_{4}\right)}{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{s}-p_{3}-p_{4}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{s}+p_{3}-p_{4}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{s}-p_{1}+p_{2}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{s}+p_{1}+p_{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{t}+p_{2}-p_{3}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{t}+p_{2}+p_{3}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{t}-p_{1}-p_{4}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{t}+p_{1}-p_{4}\right)}{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{s}-p_{3}-p_{4}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{s}+p_{3}-p_{4}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{s}-p_{1}+p_{2}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{s}+p_{1}+p_{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(Q+2p_{s}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(Q-2p_{s}\right)}{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(2p_{t}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(-2p_{t}\right)}\int_{i\mathbb{R}}\frac{\text{d}t}{i}J^{(\mathrm{ee})}_{p_{s}p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{3}&p_{2}\\
p_{4}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}_{p_{s}p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{3}&p_{2}\\
p_{4}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{e}}\text{}_{\mathrm{o}}=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{t}+p_{2}-p_{3}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{t}+p_{2}+p_{3}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{t}-p_{1}-p_{4}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{t}+p_{1}-p_{4}\right)}{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{s}-p_{3}-p_{4}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{s}+p_{3}-p_{4}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{s}-p_{1}+p_{2}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+p_{s}+p_{1}+p_{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{t}+p_{2}-p_{3}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{t}+p_{2}+p_{3}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{t}-p_{1}-p_{4}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{t}+p_{1}-p_{4}\right)}{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{s}-p_{3}-p_{4}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{s}+p_{3}-p_{4}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{s}-p_{1}+p_{2}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-p_{s}+p_{1}+p_{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(Q+2p_{s}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(Q-2p_{s}\right)}{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(2p_{t}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(-2p_{t}\right)}\int_{i\mathbb{R}}\frac{\text{d}t}{i}J^{(\mathrm{eo})}_{p_{s}p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{3}&p_{2}\\
p_{4}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]~.

(405)

The integrands
J
are given respectively by
52
52

52

As a point of reference, note that compared to the conventions of
Hadasz:2013bwa
we have shifted the contour by
t\rightarrow t-Q/2+p_{s}
.

\displaystyle J^{(\mathrm{ee})}_{p_{s}p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{3}&p_{2}\\
p_{4}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\nu=\text{NS,R}}\frac{S_{b}^{\nu}\left(t+U_{1}\right)S_{b}^{\nu}\left(t+U_{2}\right)S_{b}^{\nu}\left(t+U_{3}\right)S_{b}^{\nu}\left(t+U_{4}\right)}{S_{b}^{\nu}\left(t+V_{1}\right)S_{b}^{\nu}\left(t+V_{2}\right)S_{b}^{\nu}\left(t+V_{3}\right)S_{b}^{\nu}\left(t+V_{4}\right)},

\displaystyle J^{(\mathrm{eo})}_{p_{s}p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{3}&p_{2}\\
p_{4}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{S_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(t+U_{1}\right)S_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(t+U_{2}\right)S_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(t+U_{3}\right)S_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(t+U_{4}\right)}{S_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(t+V_{1}\right)S_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(t+V_{2}\right)S_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(t+V_{3}\right)S_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(t+V_{4}\right)}

\displaystyle~~~~-~\frac{S_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(t+U_{1}\right)S_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(t+U_{2}\right)S_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(t+U_{3}\right)S_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(t+U_{4}\right)}{S_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(t+V_{1}\right)S_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(t+V_{2}\right)S_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(t+V_{3}\right)S_{b}^{\text{R}}\left(t+V_{4}\right)},

(406)

where

\displaystyle U_{1}

\displaystyle=p_{3}-p_{4}~,\quad~~~V_{1}=\frac{Q}{2}+p_{t}+p_{2}-p_{4}~,

\displaystyle U_{2}

\displaystyle=-p_{3}-p_{4}~,~~~~V_{2}=\frac{Q}{2}-p_{t}+p_{2}-p_{4}~,

\displaystyle U_{3}

\displaystyle=p_{1}+p_{2}~,~~~~~~~V_{3}=\frac{Q}{2}+p_{s}~,

\displaystyle U_{4}

\displaystyle=-p_{1}+p_{2}~,\quad~~V_{4}=\frac{Q}{2}-p_{s}~.

The expressions for the remaining two components
\mathbb{F}_{p_{s}p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{3}&p_{2}\\
p_{4}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{o}}\text{}_{\mathrm{e}},\mathbb{F}_{p_{s}p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{3}&p_{2}\\
p_{4}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{o}}\text{}_{\mathrm{o}}
can be found e.g. in
Poghosyan:2016kvd
and we will not need them here.

In particular, we will be interested in the fusion of the identity block in the s-channel of pairwise identical external operators (i.e. when
p_{1}=p_{2}\equiv p_{1}
and
p_{3}=p_{4}\equiv p_{2}
) which is an even block
Poghosyan:2016kvd
. We then have

\mathcal{F}_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathrm{e}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}&p_{1}\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right](z)=\int_{i\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{\text{d}p_{t}}{i}\sum_{\rho=\mathrm{e},\mathrm{o}}\mathbb{F}_{\mathds{1},p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}&p_{1}\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{e}}\text{}_{\rho}\ \mathcal{F}_{p_{t}}^{\rho}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{1}&p_{1}\\
p_{2}&p_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right](1-z)~.

(407)

It is a small calculation to start from the expressions (
),(
405
) and compute the identity limit:

\ p_{2}\rightarrow p_{1}+\delta,\ p_{3}\rightarrow p_{2}-\delta\ ,\ p_{4}\equiv p_{2},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{as}\ \ p_{s}\rightarrow\frac{Q}{2}-\delta,\ \delta\rightarrow 0.

(408)

We find

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}_{\mathds{1},p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}&p_{1}\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{e}}\text{}_{\mathrm{e}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{t})C_{\mathrm{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{t},p_{1},p_{2})\ ,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}_{\mathds{1},p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}&p_{1}\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{e}}\text{}_{\mathrm{o}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2i}\rho^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{t})\widetilde{C}_{\mathrm{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{t},p_{1},p_{2})~.

(409)

The quantities
\rho_{\text{NS}}
,
C_{\mathrm{NS}},\widetilde{C}_{\mathrm{NS}}
are exactly the NS structure constants of Liouville theory given in (
135
). This relation establishes the generalization of the result given in
Teschner:2001rv
;
Collier:2019weq
, for the case of bosonic Liouville’s structure constant and the Virasoro fusion kernel.

It is worth mentioning how the calculation leading up to this result actually works, since a careful analysis can give us slightly more general formulae than (
409
) which, to the extent of our knowledge, have not appeared before in the literature and are interesting in their own rights. In other words, we will see that there is a simplification of the fusion kernel in the limit (
408
) even for finite
\delta\neq 0
.

The logic is as follows. In the limit (
408
), and
before
taking
p_{s}\rightarrow\frac{Q}{2}-\delta
or
\delta\rightarrow 0
, we find for the ‘e-e’ component

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}_{p_{s},p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}-\delta&p_{1}+\delta\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{e}}\text{}_{\mathrm{e}}=\rho^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{t})C_{\mathrm{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{t},p_{1},p_{2})\times\frac{f(p_{s};p_{i})}{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(Q/2-\delta-p_{s}\right)}\int_{i\mathbb{R}}\frac{\text{d}t}{i}J^{(ee)}_{p_{s}p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}-\delta&p_{1}+\delta\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right],

(410)

where
f
is a meromorphic function of the momenta that is regular as
p_{s}\rightarrow p_{s}^{*}\equiv Q/2-\delta
. It specifically reads

\displaystyle f(p_{s};p_{i}):=\frac{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(Q\right)^{3}\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(Q\pm 2p_{1}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(Q\pm 2p_{2}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}\pm p_{t}+p_{1}-p_{2}+2\delta\right)}{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(2Q\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}\pm p_{t}+p_{2}-p_{1}\right)}

\displaystyle~~~~~\times\frac{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(Q\pm 2p_{s}\right)}{\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}\pm p_{s}+2p_{1}+\delta\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}\pm p_{s}-2p_{2}+\delta\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+\delta\pm p_{s}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\text{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}-\delta+p_{s}\right)}~.

(411)

We therefore see that the prefactor in (
410
) has a
simple zero
when
p_{s}\rightarrow p_{s}^{*}
, even for finite
\delta\neq 0
. We hold on to that observation.

Next, for the integrand we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{i}J^{(\mathrm{ee})}_{p_{s}p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}-\delta&p_{1}+\delta\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\nu=\text{NS,R}}\frac{S_{b}^{\mathrm{\nu}}\left(t\pm\delta\right)S_{b}^{\mathrm{\nu}}\left(t+\delta+2p_{1}\right)S_{b}^{\mathrm{\nu}}\left(t+\delta-2p_{2}\right)}{i\times S_{b}^{\mathrm{\nu}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+t\pm p_{s}\right)S_{b}^{\mathrm{\nu}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+t+\delta\pm p_{t}+p_{1}-p_{2}\right)}.

(412)

In particular, the first summand in this expression only includes the functions
S_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}
, while the second only
S_{b}^{\mathrm{R}}
’s. We recall (cf. Appendix
A
) that
S_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}(x)
has a simple pole when
x=0
, whereas for
x=Q
has a simple zero. At these two points, on the other hand, the function
S_{b}^{\mathrm{R}}
is regular. Therefore, when
p_{s}\rightarrow p_{s}^{*}
the full integral develops a
pinching singularity
since the contour must pass between two colliding poles originating from the first summand in (
412
): a single pole at
t=\delta
coming from the factor
S_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(t-\delta\right)
and a single pole at
t=Q/2-p_{s}
coming from the factor
S_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}+t+p_{s}\right)
. The singularity can be evaluated by the residue of the latter. Using the fact that
\text{Res}_{x\rightarrow 0}\Gamma_{b}(x)=\frac{\Gamma_{b}(Q)}{2\pi}
, we deform appropriately and obtain for the full integral

\displaystyle\frac{S_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q/2-p_{s}\pm\delta\right)S_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q/2-p_{s}+\delta+2p_{1}\right)S_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q/2-p_{s}+\delta-2p_{2}\right)}{S_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q-2p_{s}\right)S_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q-p_{s}+\delta\pm p_{t}+p_{1}-p_{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\int_{i\mathbb{R}}\text{d}t\ \left(\text{reg. at $t\rightarrow Q/2-p_{s}$}\right).

(413)

Crucially, the first factor above has a
simple pole
at
p_{s}=p_{s}^{*}
reflecting exactly the pinching singularity that we mentioned before. With these expressions at hand, we now take the limit
p_{s}\rightarrow p_{s}^{*}
in the full kernel (prefactor times integral), in which case the simple zero of the prefactor that we encountered before will
cancel
with the simple pole from the pinching singularity, and this will end up giving us the following general simple expression for the ‘e-e’ component of the full kernel:

\mathbb{F}_{Q/2-\delta,p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}-\delta&p_{1}+\delta\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{e}}\text{}_{\mathrm{e}}=\zeta^{(e)}_{\delta}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{t})\times\mathbb{F}_{\mathds{1},p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}&p_{1}\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{e}}\text{}_{\mathrm{e}}\ ,\ \ \ \ \ \delta\neq 0

(414)

where

\displaystyle\zeta^{(\mathrm{e})}_{\delta}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{t}):=

(415)

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(2Q-2\delta\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q-2p_{1}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q+2p_{2}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}\pm p_{t}+p_{2}-p_{1}-2\delta\right)}{\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(2Q\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q-2\delta\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q-2p_{1}-2\delta\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q+2p_{2}-2\delta\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}\pm p_{t}+p_{2}-p_{1}\right)}~,

(416)

and

\mathbb{F}_{\mathds{1},p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}&p_{1}\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{e}}\text{}_{\mathrm{e}}:=\lim_{\delta\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{F}_{Q/2-\delta,p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}-\delta&p_{1}+\delta\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{e}}\text{}_{\mathrm{e}}=\rho^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{t})C_{\mathrm{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{t},p_{1},p_{2}).

(417)

The last equation is exactly what we wanted to establish, but equation (
414
) is more interesting since it highlights a relation of
\rho_{\mathrm{NS}},C_{\mathrm{NS}}
with a more general fusion kernel entry with
\delta\neq 0
.

Doing similar manipulations for the ‘e-o’ component, we find

\mathbb{F}_{Q/2-\delta,p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}-\delta&p_{1}+\delta\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{e}}\text{}_{\mathrm{o}}=\zeta^{(\mathrm{o})}_{\delta}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{t})\times\mathbb{F}_{\mathds{1},p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}&p_{1}\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{e}}\text{}_{\mathrm{o}}\ ,\ \ \ \ \ \delta\neq 0

(418)

where

\displaystyle\zeta^{(\mathrm{o})}_{\delta}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{t}):=

(419)

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(2Q-2\delta\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q-2p_{1}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q+2p_{2}\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{R}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}\pm p_{t}+p_{2}-p_{1}-2\delta\right)}{\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(2Q\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q-2\delta\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q-2p_{1}-2\delta\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{NS}}\left(Q+2p_{2}-2\delta\right)\Gamma_{b}^{\mathrm{R}}\left(\frac{Q}{2}\pm p_{t}+p_{2}-p_{1}\right)},

(420)

and

\mathbb{F}_{\mathds{1},p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}&p_{1}\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{e}}\text{}_{\mathrm{o}}:=\lim_{\delta\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{F}_{Q/2-\delta,p_{t}}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}p_{2}-\delta&p_{1}+\delta\\
p_{2}&p_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\mathrm{e}}\text{}_{\mathrm{o}}=\frac{1}{2i}\rho^{(b)}_{\text{NS}}(p_{t})\widetilde{C}_{\mathrm{NS}}^{(b)}(p_{t},p_{1},p_{2}).

(421)

The last equation is again what we wanted to show, although (
418
) holds more generally for
\delta\neq 0
.
